\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}>\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{1}+C^{\natural},\ \ \tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{4}>\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{4}+C^{\natural},\ \ \tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{\mathcal{NP}}>\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{\mathcal{NP}}+C^{\natural},\\[2.0pt]
&\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}>K^{(\tau)}_{r,i}\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{i},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}_{c}>\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}K^{(\tau)}_{b}\qquad\mbox{for $i=2,3,4$},\end{split}

(2.52)

constant
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}>0
will be determined later,

C^{\natural}>0
is a large constant depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
,
and coefficients
K^{(\tau)}_{b}
,
K^{(\tau)}_{r,i},\ i=2,3,4
, are given by Lemmas
2.4
–
2.5
.

Then we show that
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x)
is decreasing by dividing four cases:

Case 1
:
Interactions between two physical wave-fronts at
(x^{\natural},\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}))
.

Without loss of generality, consider the
1^{\rm th}
wave-front
\alpha_{1}
from the region above
y=\mathcal{Y}(x)

interacts with the
4^{\rm th}
wave-front
\alpha_{4}
from the region below
y=\mathcal{Y}(x)
at
(x^{\natural},\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}))
.
If the resulting wave-fronts are all physical, which are denoted by
\beta_{j}
for
j=1,2,3,4
,
then, by Lemma
2.5
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{j}}+O(1)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|\,\,\,\,\mbox{for $j=1,4$},\quad\,\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{i}}=O(1)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|\,\,\,\,\mbox{for $i=2,3$}.

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,4}\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{i}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),a}_{i}(x^{\natural}+)-\sum_{i=1,4}\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{i}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),a}_{i}(x^{\natural}-)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{4}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|+C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,4}\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{i}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),b}_{i}(x^{\natural}+)-\sum_{i=1,4}\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{i}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),b}_{i}(x^{\natural}-)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{1}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{4}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|+C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),a}_{\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),a}_{\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}(x^{\natural}-)=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),b}_{\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),b}_{\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}(x^{\natural}-)=0,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2,3}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{i}(x^{\natural}+)-\sum_{i=2,3}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{i}(x^{\natural}-)\leq C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{c}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{c}(x^{\natural}-)=0,

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}(x^{\natural}-)&=|\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\natural}+,\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}+))-\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\natural}-,\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}-))|\\[2.0pt]
&\quad\ +|p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\natural}+,\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}+))-p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\natural}-,\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}-))|\\[2.0pt]
&\leq C\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}|\big)\\[2.0pt]
&\leq C\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|\big)+C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,\\[2.0pt]
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)&\leq\big(-1+C\,\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|\leq-\frac{1}{2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,\end{split}

when
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)
is sufficiently small.

Therefore, choosing
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}>0
sufficiently large and the coefficients as in (
2.52
),
we can obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\\
&\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}+C\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|+\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{4}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{4}+C\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|\\
&\quad+\big(-\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}}{2}+C\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|<0,\end{split}

by choosing
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}>0
sufficiently large.

If a nonphysical wave-front
\beta_{\mathcal{NP}}
is generated, then, by Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}},\quad\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}},\quad\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{\mathcal{NP}}}=O(1)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|.

Thus, for
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)
sufficiently small, we can follow the above procedures to deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)&\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}+C\,\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|\\[2.0pt]
&\quad\,+\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{4}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{4}+C\,\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|+C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,\\[2.0pt]
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)&\leq\big(-1+C\,\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|\leq-\frac{1}{2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|,\end{split}

which implies

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)<0,

by letting
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}
sufficiently large and the coefficients as in (
2.52
).

Case 2
:
Interactions involving nonphysical wave-fronts at
(x^{\natural},\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}))
.

Consider the
1^{\rm st}
wave-front
\alpha_{1}
from the region above
y=\mathcal{Y}(x)

interacts with the nonphysical wave-front
\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}
from the region below
y=\mathcal{Y}(x)

at
(x^{\natural},\mathcal{Y}(x^{\natural}))
.
Let
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{\mathcal{NP}}
be the resulting wave-fronts.
Then, by Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}},\quad\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{\mathcal{NP}}}=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}+O(1)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|.

Therefore, when
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)
is sufficiently small, a direct computation yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,4}\sum_{j=a,b}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),j}_{i}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),j}_{i}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),j}_{i}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),j}_{i}(x^{\natural}-)\big)\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=a,b}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),j}_{\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau),j}_{\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}(x^{\natural}-)\big)\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{\mathcal{NP}}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{\mathcal{NP}}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|+C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2,3}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{i}(x^{\natural}+)-\sum_{i=2,3}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{i}(x^{\natural}-)=\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{c}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{c}(x^{\natural}-)=0,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}(x^{\natural}-)\leq C\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|\big)+C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|,

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)&\leq\big(-1+C\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|\leq-\frac{1}{2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|.\end{split}

Choosing
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}>0
sufficiently large and the coefficients as in (
2.52
),
we then obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\\[2.0pt]
&\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}+C\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}|+\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{\mathcal{NP}}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),b}_{\mathcal{NP}}+C\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|\\
&\quad+\big(-\frac{\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}}{2}+C\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}|<0.\end{split}

For the other cases of interactions, we can done in the same way as above.

Case 3
:
Weak wave-fronts hit on boundary
y=g_{h}(x)
.
We only consider that a
4^{\rm th}
wave-front
\alpha_{4}
hits the approximate boundary
y=g_{h}(x)
since the other cases can be done in the same way. Let
\beta_{1}
be the reflected wave-front. Then, as shown in Lemma
2.4
, we have the relation:

\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=K^{(\tau)}_{r,4}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}
.
By direct computation, we can further obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{4}K^{(\tau)}_{r,4}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{4}}|,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)\leq C\,\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|.

Therefore, when
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)
is sufficiently small,
we choose
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}
sufficiently large and the coefficients as in (
2.52
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{4}K^{(\tau)}_{r,4}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}+C\,\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|<0.\end{split}

Case 4
:
The
1^{\rm st}
wave-front
\alpha_{1}
issues from the corner point
(x_{k},g_{h}(x_{k}))
of
boundary
y=g_{h}(x)
. As shown in Lemma
2.6
, we see that

\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{1}}=K^{(\tau)}_{b}\omega_{k}
.
Similar to the case above, for
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)
sufficiently small,
we choose
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}
sufficiently large and the coefficients as in (
2.52
) to obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}+)-\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\leq\big(\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau),a}_{1}K^{(\tau)}_{b}-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}_{c}+C\,\tilde{\mathcal{K}}^{(\tau)}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\natural}-)\big)|\omega_{k}|<0.

Finally, we remark that the cases of interaction of wave-fronts away from
y=\mathcal{Y}(x)
can be handled
in the same way as in Lemma
2.7
.

We conclude from the decreasing of
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}
that, for all
x>0
,

\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(x)<\tilde{\mathcal{G}}^{(\tau)}(0)\leq C\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV((0,\infty))}\big),

provided that
\varepsilon
and
\tau
are sufficiently small, where
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.
This implies the uniform bound of
\big\|(\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\big\|_{BV(\mathcal{Y}(\Sigma_{x}))}

for all
x>0
.
∎

Based on Lemma
2.8
, we have the following
L^{1}
-comparison estimate
on the flow slope function
\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}
.

Proposition 2.2
.

Suppose that
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
are the approximate solutions
of problem (
1.9
)–(
1.11
) constructed by the wave-front tracking scheme in
§2.4
.
Then, for
\varepsilon>0
and
\tau>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,g_{h}(s))\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1}))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,6}\|\check{g}_{h}(s)-g_{h}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1}))}

(2.53)

for any
x^{\natural}_{1}>x^{\natural}_{0}>0
, where
C_{2,6}>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

Let
y=\mathcal{Y}(x)
be a Lipschitz continuous curve that is a linear combination of
g_{h}(x)
and
\check{g}_{h}(x)

as described above.
Without loss of generality, we take
\mathcal{Y}(x)
in the following form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x)\doteq\vartheta g_{h}(x)+(1-\vartheta)\check{g}_{h}(x)\qquad\mbox{for $x\in(0,\infty)$ and $\vartheta\in[0,1]$}.

Choosing a positive
C^{0,1}
-function
\eta(x)
with
\|\eta\|_{C^{0,1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}
sufficiently small such that
there are only a finitely number of wave-fronts
y_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})

between
\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x)
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x)+\eta(x)
,
which divide region
(\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x),\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x)+\eta(x))
into many
subregions
with constant states, and
all the fronts are not interacted with each other.

We first consider estimate (
2.2
) for the special case
\check{g}_{h}=\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x)

and
g_{h}=\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x)+\eta(x)
.
For notational convenience, we still denote
\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}=\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}
.
Since
\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
is unchanged when it crosses wave-fronts
\alpha\in\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{2}\cup\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{3}
,
it suffices
to consider the case:

\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\setminus(\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{2}\cup\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{3})
.
To this end, let
y=y_{\alpha}(x)
be a wave-front issue
from
(x^{\natural}_{0},\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x^{\natural}_{0}))

(or
(x^{\natural}_{0},\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x^{\natural}_{0})+\eta(x^{\natural}_{0}))
) to

(x^{\natural}_{1},\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x^{\natural}_{1})+\eta(x^{\natural}_{1}))

(or
(x^{\natural}_{1},\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x^{\natural}_{1}))
).
Moreover, for
\varepsilon>0
and
\tau>0
sufficiently small,
there exists a constant
d^{\natural}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})

such that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\vartheta}(x)-y^{\prime}_{\alpha}(x)|>d^{\natural},\qquad|\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\vartheta}(x)+\eta^{\prime}(x)-y^{\prime}_{\alpha}(x)|>d^{\natural}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}d^{\natural}\,(x^{\natural}_{1}-x^{\natural}_{0})&<\int^{x^{\natural}_{1}}_{x^{\natural}_{0}}\big|\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\vartheta}(t)+\eta^{\prime}(t)-y^{\prime}_{\alpha}(t)\big|\,{\rm d}t\\[2.0pt]
&=\bigg|\int^{x^{\natural}_{1}}_{x^{\natural}_{0}}\big(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\vartheta}(t)+\eta^{\prime}(t)-y^{\prime}_{\alpha}(t)\big)\,{\rm d}t\bigg|\\[2.0pt]
&=\Big|\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x^{\natural}_{1})+\eta(x^{\natural}_{1})-y_{\alpha}(x^{\natural}_{1})-\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(x^{\natural}_{0})+\eta(x^{\natural}_{0})-y_{\alpha}(x^{\natural}_{0})\big)\Big|\\[2.0pt]
&\leq\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1}))}.\end{split}

Then it follows from the above estimate that

\displaystyle\int^{x^{\natural}_{1}}_{x^{\natural}_{0}}\big|\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(t)+\eta(t))-\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}(t))\big|\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\setminus(\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{2}\cup\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{3})}\big|\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,y_{\alpha}(t)-)-\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,y_{\alpha}(t)+)\big|(x^{\natural}_{1}-x^{\natural}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1}))}}{d^{\natural}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\setminus(\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{2}\cup\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{3})}\big|\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,y_{\alpha}(t)-)-\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,y_{\alpha}(t)+)\big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big\|(\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\big\|_{BV(\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1})))}\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1}))}.

(2.54)

Next, we turn to consider estimate (
2.2
).
Choose a sequence
\{\vartheta_{j}\}^{n}_{j=0}
in
[0,1]

with
0=\vartheta_{0}<\vartheta_{1}<\cdot\cdot\cdot<\vartheta_{n-1}<\vartheta_{n}=1

satisfying that
\vartheta_{j+1}-\vartheta_{j}
,
j=0,\cdots,n-1
, are sufficiently small when
n
is sufficiently large.
Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta_{j}}(x)\doteq\vartheta_{j}g_{h}(x)+(1-\vartheta_{j})\check{g}_{h}(x)\quad\,\,\mbox{for $x\in(0,\infty)$, $\vartheta_{j}\in[0,1]$,\ $j=0,1,\cdots,n$}.

By direct calculation, we see that, for
j=0,1,\cdots,n
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta_{j+1}}(x)-\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta_{j}}(x)\|_{C^{0,1}}=(\vartheta_{j+1}-\vartheta_{j})\|g_{h}(x)-\check{g}_{h}(x)\|_{C^{0,1}}\ll 1.

Then, applying estimate (
2.4
), we have

\displaystyle\int^{x^{\natural}_{1}}_{x^{\natural}_{0}}\big|\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,\check{g}_{h}(t))-\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,g_{h}(t))\big|\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum^{n}_{j=0}\int^{x^{\natural}_{1}}_{x^{\natural}_{0}}\big|\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta_{j+1}}(t))-\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(t,\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta_{j}}(t))\big|\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sum^{n}_{j=0}(\vartheta_{j+1}-\vartheta_{j})\big\|(\omega^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\big\|_{BV(\mathcal{Y}_{\vartheta_{j}}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1})))}\|\check{g}_{h}(s)-g_{h}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}((x^{\natural}_{0},x^{\natural}_{1}))},

(2.55)

where
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Therefore, using estimate (
2.51
) in Lemma
2.8
and estimate (
2.4
),
we can finally derive inequality (
2.2
) by choosing a constant
C_{2,6}>0
that depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.
∎

2.5
Proof of Theorem 1.1 for Problem I

In this subsection, we first establish the
L^{1}
–stability estimate of the approximate solutions
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}

of problem (
1.9
)–(
1.11
) constructed by the wave-front tracking scheme in §2.4.
Then we further show the convergence of the approximate solutions
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}

in
L^{1}
as
h\rightarrow 0
and
\nu\rightarrow\infty
, whose limit is the entropy solution of Problem I.

To establish the
L^{1}
–stability estimate, we introduce a Lyapunov functional

\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})
, which is equivalent to the
L^{1}
–distance between
two approximate solutions:

U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}=\big(\rho^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}\big)^{\top},\qquad V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}=\big(\hat{\rho}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu},\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu},\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu},\hat{p}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}\big)^{\top},

corresponding to the initial data and boundaries
(U^{\nu}_{0},g_{h})
and
(V^{\nu}_{0},g_{h^{\prime}})
, respectively.

First, we connect states
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}
in the phase space along the Hugoniot
curves
\mathcal{S}^{(\tau)}_{1}
,
\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{2}
,
\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{3}
, and
\mathcal{S}^{(\tau)}_{4}
:

\displaystyle V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)};U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle\doteq\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}_{4}(q^{(\tau)}_{4};\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}_{3}(q^{(\tau)}_{3};\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}_{2}(q^{(\tau)}_{2};\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}_{1}(q^{(\tau)}_{1};U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}),

(2.56)

where
\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}=(q^{(\tau)}_{1},q^{(\tau)}_{2},q^{(\tau)}_{3},q^{(\tau)}_{4})
is the Hugoniot wave vector.
Then, due to the boundary effect, we modify
\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}
by imposing some weights:

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}=\begin{cases}q^{(\tau)}_{j}&\ \,\,\mbox{for $j=1$},\\[5.0pt]
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{j}q^{(\tau)}_{j}&\ \,\,\mbox{for $2\leq j\leq 4$},\end{cases}

(2.57)

where
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{j}>0,j=2,3,4
, will be determined later.
Set

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{h}(x)=\max\big\{g_{h}(x),g_{h^{\prime}}(x)\big\},\qquad\check{g}_{h}(x)=\min\big\{g_{h}(x),g_{h^{\prime}}(x)\big\}.

(2.58)

Then the Lyapunov functional is given as

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}\big(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}\big)(x)

\displaystyle=\sum^{4}_{j=1}\int^{\check{g}_{h}(x)}_{-\infty}|\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)\,{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}\int^{\infty}_{x}\Big|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-\Big(\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))\Big|\,{\rm d}s,

(2.59)

where

\displaystyle W^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=1+\kappa_{1}A^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)+\kappa^{(\tau)}_{2}\big(\mathcal{Q}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})+\mathcal{Q}^{(\tau)}(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})\big)

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\kappa^{(\tau)}_{3}\sum_{\alpha_{4}\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{4}}|+\kappa^{(\tau)}_{c}\sum_{k>[\frac{x}{h}]}\omega_{k}+\kappa^{(\tau)^{\prime}}_{c}\sum_{k^{\prime}>[\frac{x}{h^{\prime}}]}\omega^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

(2.60)

with constants
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{c}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)^{\prime}}_{c}
, and
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{j},j=1,2,3
, to be determined later.
The functional,
\mathcal{Q}^{(\tau)}
, represents the total interaction potential with respect to
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}

as defined in (
2.42
), and
\omega_{k}
and
\omega^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
represent the turning angles at the corner points
(x_{k},g_{h}(x_{k}))
and
(x_{k^{\prime}},g_{h^{\prime}}(x_{k^{\prime}}))
respectively.
Finally,

A^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)
, which measures the total strength of the waves in
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
V^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
approaching to the
j^{\rm th}
Hugoniot
wave
q^{(\tau)}_{j}
at
y=y_{\alpha_{i}}
, is defined by

\displaystyle A^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\cup\mathcal{J}(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})\atop y_{\alpha}<y,j<i_{\alpha}\leq 4}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}|+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\cup\mathcal{J}(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})\atop y_{\alpha}>y,1\leq i_{\alpha}<j}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}|

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\begin{cases}\bigg(\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\atop y<y_{\alpha},i_{\alpha}=j}+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})\atop y>y_{\alpha},i_{\alpha}=j}\bigg)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}|\qquad\text{if}\ q^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)<0,\\[15.0pt]
\bigg(\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})\atop y>y_{\alpha},i_{\alpha}=j}+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\atop y<y_{\alpha},i_{\alpha}=j}\bigg)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}|\qquad\text{if}\ q^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)>0,\end{cases}

(2.61)

where
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}
is the strength of the weak wave
\alpha
of the
i_{\alpha}^{\rm th}
characteristic family.

By (
2.47
) in Proposition
2.1
, we can take
\varepsilon
sufficiently small such that

1<W^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)<2
for
1\leq j\leq 4
. Therefore, we can choose a constant
C_{2,7}>0
, independent of
(h,\nu,\tau)
, so that

\displaystyle C_{2,7}^{-1}\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{4}_{j=1}\int^{\check{g}_{h}(x)}_{-\infty}|\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(y)\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,7}\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}}.

(2.62)

Next, we analyze the Lyapunov functional along the flow direction
x>0
.
To achieve this, we first give the following lemma.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let two constant states

U^{(\tau)}(b)=\big(\rho^{(\tau)},u^{(\tau)},v^{(\tau)},p^{(\tau)}\big)^{\top}(b)

and
V^{(\tau)}(b^{\prime})=\big(\hat{\rho}^{(\tau)},\hat{u}^{(\tau)},\hat{v}^{(\tau)},\hat{p}^{(\tau)}\big)^{\top}(b^{\prime})

satisfy

\displaystyle V^{(\tau)}(b^{\prime})=\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b);U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2}),

(2.63)

and

\displaystyle(1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}(b),v^{(\tau)}(b))\cdot\mathbf{n}_{k}=0,\ \ \ (1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}(b^{\prime}),\hat{v}^{(\tau)}(b^{\prime}))\cdot\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=0,

(2.64)

where

\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b)=(q^{(\tau)}_{1},q^{(\tau)}_{2},q^{(\tau)}_{3},q^{(\tau)}_{4})(b)
,
\mathbf{n}_{k}=(\sin\theta_{k},-\cos\theta_{k})
,
and
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=(\sin\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},-\cos\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})
.
Then there exist constants
\varepsilon_{3}>0
and
\tau_{2}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
such that,
if
U^{(\tau)}_{b},\hat{U}^{(\tau)}_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\underline{U})
,
and
|\theta_{k}|+|\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}|<\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{3})
and
\tau\in(0,\tau_{2})
, then

\displaystyle q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)=\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\tau^{2})q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)+\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}(\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-\theta_{k}),

(2.65)

where the bounds of
\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\tau^{2})
and
\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}

depend only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

According to (
2.63
)–(
2.64
), we denote

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})

\displaystyle:=\big(1+\tau^{2}\mathcal{H}^{(\tau),(2)}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{b};U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})\big)\sin(\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\,\quad\ -\mathcal{H}^{(\tau),(3)}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b);U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})\cos(\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}).

Notice that

\mathbb{L}_{b}(0,0,\underline{U},\tau^{2})=0
and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})}{\partial q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)}\Bigg|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{r}^{(\tau),3}_{1}(\underline{U},\tau^{2})=-\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{2}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}.

Then we can choose
\tau^{\prime}_{2}>0
depending only on
a_{\infty}
such that, for
\tau\in(0,\tau^{\prime}_{2})
,

\frac{\partial\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})}{\partial q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)}\Bigg|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}<0.

Therefore, by the implicit function theorem, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, the equation:

\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})=0

admits a unique solution
q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)=q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},\tau^{2})

that is a
C^{2}
–function of
(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},\tau^{2})
.
Denote

q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b)\doteq q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta_{k},\tau^{2}).

Then, by the Taylor formula, we have

\displaystyle q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},\tau^{2})=q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b)+\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}(\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-\theta_{k}),

(2.66)

where coefficient
\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}
is a
C^{2}
–function of

(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},\tau^{2})
.
Moreover, by direct computation, coefficient
\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}\Big|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\frac{\partial\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}\Big|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}}{\frac{\partial\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b),\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})}{\partial q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)}\Big|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{2}}.

This implies that
\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}
is uniformly bounded if
\tau\in(0,\tau^{\prime\prime}_{2})

with
\tau^{\prime\prime}_{2}>0
small and depending only on
a_{\infty}
.
Thus, we choose
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{3}>0
such that, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{3})
,
if
U^{(\tau)}_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\underline{U})
and
|\theta_{k}|+|\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}|<\varepsilon
,
then the bound of
\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}
is independent of

(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Next, we estimate
q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b)
. Let

\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b)=(q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)),\quad V^{(\tau)}(b)=\big(\hat{\rho}^{(\tau)},\hat{u}^{(\tau)},\hat{v}^{(\tau)},\hat{p}^{(\tau)}\big)^{\top}(b).

Then

\displaystyle V^{(\tau)}(b)=\mathcal{H}^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b);U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2}),\quad(1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}(b),\hat{v}^{(\tau)}(b))\cdot\mathbf{n}_{k}=0.\quad

(2.67)

Let
U^{(\tau)}_{i}(b)=(\rho^{(\tau)}_{i},u^{(\tau)}_{i},v^{(\tau)}_{i},p^{(\tau)}_{i})^{\top}(b)
,
i=1,2,3
,
be the middle states of the solution of problem (
2.67
) from
U^{(\tau)}(b)
to
V^{(\tau)}(b)
.
If
q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)=0
, then
V^{(\tau)}(b)=U^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)
.
Thus, by the boundary conditions (
2.64
) and (
2.67
),
we see that

\displaystyle\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)}=\frac{v^{(\tau)}(b)}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}(b)}.

(2.68)

On the other hand, along the
2^{\rm nd}
wave curve
\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle u^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)=\big(1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big)e^{\tau^{2}q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)},\quad v^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)=v^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)e^{\tau^{2}q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)},

and
\rho^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)=\rho^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)
and
p^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)=p^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)
.
Along the
3^{\rm rd}
wave curve
\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\rho^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)=\rho^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)+q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),\,\,u^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)=u^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),\,\,v^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)=v^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),\,\,p^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)=p^{(\tau)}_{2}(b).

This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{3}(b)}=\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)e^{\tau^{2}q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)}}{\big(1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big)e^{\tau^{2}q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b)}}=\frac{\mathcal{H}^{(\tau),3}(q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b);U^{(\tau)}(b))}{1+\tau^{2}\mathcal{H}^{(\tau),2}(q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b);U^{(\tau)}(b))}.\quad\,\,

(2.69)

Then it follows from (
2.68
)–(
2.69
) that

q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b)\Big|_{q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)=0}=0,

so that applying the Taylor formula again yields

\displaystyle q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b)=\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta_{k},\tau^{2})q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta_{k},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\int^{1}_{0}\partial_{\mu}q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)(q^{(\tau)}_{2}(b),q^{(\tau)}_{3}(b),\mu q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b),\theta_{k},\tau^{2})\,{\rm d}\mu,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}\Big|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta_{k}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}=-\frac{\frac{\partial\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b),\theta_{k},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})}{\partial q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)}\Big|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta_{k}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}}{\frac{\partial\mathbb{L}_{b}(\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b),\theta_{k},U^{(\tau)}(b),\tau^{2})}{\partial q^{(\tau)}_{1*}(b)}\Big|_{\boldsymbol{q}^{(\tau)}_{*}(b)=\boldsymbol{0},\theta_{k}=0,U^{(\tau)}(b)=\underline{U}}}=-1.

Therefore, we can choose
\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}>0
depending only on
a_{\infty}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{3}>0
such that,
for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{3})
and
\tau\in(0,\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2})
,
if
U^{(\tau)}_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\underline{U})
and
|\theta_{k}|+|\theta^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}|<\varepsilon
,
then the bound of
\mathbb{K}_{b}
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Finally, let
\varepsilon_{3}=\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{3},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{3}\}
and
\tilde{\tau}_{0}=\min\{\tau^{\prime}_{2},\tau^{\prime\prime}_{2},\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}\}
.
Then, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{3})
and
\tau\in(0,\tau_{2})
, we obtain the desired results.
∎

Now we can analyze the Lyapunov functional along the flow direction
x>0
.

Lemma 2.10
.

Suppose that
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}
are the approximate solutions
corresponding to the initial data and boundaries
(U^{\nu}_{0},g_{h})
and
(V^{\nu}_{0},g_{h^{\prime}})
, respectively,
and satisfy the properties listed in
Proposition
2.1
.
Then there exist suitable positive constants
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{c}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)^{\prime}}_{c}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}
,

\kappa^{(\tau)}_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
, and
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{j}
for
j=2,3,4
,
depending only on
\underline{U}
, such that functional
\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}
defined by (
2.5
) satisfies
the estimate
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}\big(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}\big)(x^{\prime\prime})\leq\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}\big(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}\big)(x^{\prime})+C\big(x^{\prime\prime}-x^{\prime}\big)\nu^{-1}

(2.70)

for any
0<x^{\prime}<x^{\prime\prime}
, where constant
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

Denote by
U^{(\tau)}_{0}\doteq U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,y),U^{(\tau)}_{1},\ U^{(\tau)}_{2},\ U^{(\tau)}_{3}
,
and
U^{(\tau)}_{4}\doteq V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,y)

the intermediate states of the Riemann solution of equation (
2.5
).
Let
\lambda^{(\tau)}_{i}\doteq\lambda_{i}(U^{(\tau)}_{i-1},U^{(\tau)}_{i-1})
,
1\leq i\leq 4
,
be the corresponding speed of the
i^{\rm th}
wave
q^{(\tau)}_{i}
along the Hugoniot curve in the phase space.
Let
\check{g}^{-}_{h}(x)\doteq b>y_{1}>y_{2}>\cdot\cdot\cdot>y_{N}
with
(x,y_{l})
as
the jump point of
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}
for
1\leq l\leq N
,
and let
\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}=\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})\cup\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})

be the set of indices
\alpha\in\{1,2,\cdot\cdot\cdot,N\}
.
We define

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha\pm}_{i}=\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{i}(y^{\pm}_{\alpha}),\quad\,W^{(\tau),\alpha\pm}_{i}=W^{(\tau)}_{i}(y^{\pm}_{\alpha}),\quad\,\lambda^{(\tau),\alpha\pm}_{i}=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{i}(y^{\pm}_{\alpha}).

(2.71)

When there is no wave interaction, no wave reflection, or no corner point on the boundary at
x
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(x)}{{\rm d}x}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}\sum^{4}_{j=1}\Big(|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}-|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}\Big)\dot{y}_{\alpha}+\sum^{4}_{j=1}|\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\dot{y}_{b}\quad

\displaystyle\quad\,-\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}\bigg|\bigg(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\bigg)(x,\check{g}_{h}(x))\bigg|,

(2.72)

where
\dot{y}_{\alpha}
is the speed of the weak wave
\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}
,
b=\check{g}^{-}_{h}(x)
,
and
\dot{y}_{b}=\check{g}_{h}^{\prime}(x)
.
Notice that

\displaystyle|\hat{q}^{(\tau),1+}_{j}|W^{(\tau),1+}_{j}\lambda^{(\tau),1+}_{j}=|\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(b),

\displaystyle|\hat{q}^{(\tau),(\alpha-1)-}_{j}|W^{(\tau),(\alpha-1)-}_{j}\lambda^{(\tau),(\alpha-1)-}_{j}=|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}\lambda^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}.

Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}\sum^{4}_{j=1}|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}\lambda^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}

\displaystyle\ =\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}\sum^{4}_{j=1}|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}\lambda^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}-\sum^{4}_{j=1}|\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(b).

(2.73)

Substituting (
2.5
) into (
2.5
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(x)}{{\rm d}x}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}\sum^{4}_{j=1}\Big(|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{j}\big(\dot{y}_{\alpha}-\lambda^{(\tau),\alpha-}_{i}\big)-|\hat{q}^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}|W^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{j}\big(\dot{y}_{\alpha}-\lambda^{(\tau),\alpha+}_{i}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\ +\sum^{4}_{j=1}|\hat{q}^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,-\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}\bigg|\bigg(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\bigg)(x,\check{g}_{h}(x))\bigg|

\displaystyle\doteq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{\alpha,j}+\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{b,j}-\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}\bigg|\bigg(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\bigg)(x,\check{g}_{h}(x))\bigg|.

(2.74)

Following the standard arguments
in
[
4
]
,
if
\alpha\in\mathcal{S}^{(\tau)}\cup\mathcal{C}^{(\tau)}\cup\mathcal{R}^{(\tau)}\cup\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}
,
then we choose
\kappa_{1}
sufficiently large to obtain

\displaystyle\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{\alpha,j}\leq C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}|\nu^{-1}\qquad\mbox{or}\qquad\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{\alpha,j}\leq C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha}|,

(2.75)

where constant
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.
Therefore, the remaining task is to estimate
\sum^{4}_{i=1}E^{(\tau)}_{b,i}
involving the approximate boundary
\check{\Gamma}_{h}
.

\bullet
If
\check{g}_{h}(x)=g_{h}(x)
, then, by Lemma
2.9
, we have

\displaystyle\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{b,j}

\displaystyle=|q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big)+\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}|q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\big)

\displaystyle\quad\ +\sum^{4}_{j=3}\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{j}|q^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(|\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}|W^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(b))+|\text{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}|W^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{4}(b))\Big)|q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)|

\displaystyle\quad\ +|\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}|W^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\,\,\,\times\Big|\Big(\arctan(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}})-\arctan(\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}})\Big)(x,g_{h}(x))\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\ +\sum^{4}_{j=3}\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{j}|q^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)|W^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)\big)

\displaystyle\doteq J_{1,b}+J_{2,b}+J_{3,b}.

Since

\displaystyle\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\Big|_{U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}=V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}=\underline{U}}=-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\Big|_{U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}=V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}=\underline{U}}

at the background states when
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(b)=V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(b)=\underline{U}
and
\dot{y}_{b}=0
,
we find that, for the coefficient function in
J_{1,b}
,
if
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}>0
sufficiently large and
\tau>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\Big(|\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}|W^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)\big)+|\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}|W^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\big(\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\big)\Big)\Big|_{U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(b)=V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(b)=\underline{U},\dot{y}_{b}=0}

\displaystyle=\Big(|\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{b}|W^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)-|\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}|W^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\Big)\lambda^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)\Big|_{U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(b)=V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(b)=\underline{U},\dot{y}_{b}=0}

\displaystyle\leq-C_{2,8},

where constant
C_{2,8}>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Therefore, if
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}>0
is large enough, depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
,
and if
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}\})
and
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\})
,
there exists a constant
C_{2,9}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
such that

\displaystyle J_{1,b}\leq-C_{2,9}|q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)|.

For
J_{2,b}
, if
\varepsilon>0
and
\tau>0
are sufficiently small, we obtain

\displaystyle J_{2,b}\leq C\bigg|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(x,g_{h}(x))\bigg|.

For
J_{3,b}
, note that, when
j=2,3
, it follows from Lemma
2.9
that

\displaystyle\dot{y}_{b}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(b)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(|q^{(\tau)}_{1}(b)|+|q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C|q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)|+O(1)\bigg|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(x,g_{h}(x))\bigg|.

Inserting this estimate into
J_{3,b}
to obtain

\displaystyle J_{3,b}\leq C\big(\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{2}+\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{3}\big)\bigg(|q^{(\tau)}_{4}(b)|+\bigg|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(x,g_{h}(x))\bigg|\bigg).

Finally, by letting
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}>0
and
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}
sufficiently large
and choosing
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{2}
and
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{3}
appropriately,
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
,
it follows from the estimates of
J_{1,b}
,
J_{2,b}
, and
J_{3,b}
that

\displaystyle\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{b,j}-\kappa^{(\tau)}_{g}\bigg|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(x,g_{h}(x))\bigg|<0.

(2.76)

\bullet
If
\check{g}_{h}(x)=g_{h^{\prime}}(x)
, this can done in the same way as done for the first case.

\bullet
If
\check{g}_{h}(x)=g_{h}(x)=g_{h^{\prime}}(x)
, then it follows from Lemma
2.9
that

\mathbb{K}^{(\tau)}_{\theta}=0
. Then
J_{2,b}\equiv 0
.
Now, choosing
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{4}>0
sufficiently large and choosing
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{2}

and
\textsc{w}^{(\tau)}_{3}
appropriately, we obtain that
\sum^{4}_{j=1}E^{(\tau)}_{b,j}<0
.

Thus, inequality (
2.76
) holds for all the three subcases above.
Then, by estimates (
2.75
)–(
2.76
), we see from equality (
2.5
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}<C\nu^{-1}.

(2.77)

To complete the proof of this lemma, let
\check{x}_{1}<\check{x}_{2}<\cdot\cdot\cdot<\check{x}_{N}
be the points in
(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})

such that, when
x=\check{x}_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq N
, the fronts in
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
or
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}
interact,
reflect on the boundary, or are generated by the corner points on the boundary.
We integrate inequality (
2.75
) along the flow direction
on each of intervals
(x^{\prime},\check{x}_{1})
,
(\check{x}_{1},\check{x}_{2})
,
\cdots
,

(\check{x}_{N},x^{\prime\prime})
to obtain

\begin{split}&\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(\check{x}_{1}-)<\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(x^{\prime})+C(\check{x}_{1}-x^{\prime})\nu^{-1},\\[2.0pt]
&\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(\check{x}_{2}-)<\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(\check{x}_{1}+)+C(\check{x}_{2}-\check{x}_{1})\nu^{-1},\\[2.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot\\[2.0pt]
&\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(x)<\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(\check{x}_{N}+)+C(x^{\prime\prime}-\check{x}_{N})\nu^{-1},\qquad\end{split}

(2.78)

where
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.
When
x=\check{x}_{k}
, for
k=1,\cdot\cdot\cdot,N
,
choosing
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{1}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{2}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{3}
,
\kappa^{(\tau)}_{c}
, and
\kappa^{(\tau)^{\prime}}_{c}
sufficiently large and
using Lemma
2.7
, we have

\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(\check{x}_{k}+)<\mathscr{L}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})(\check{x}_{k}-)\quad\,\,\mbox{for $k=1,\cdot\cdot\cdot,N$}.\,\,\,

(2.79)

Combining estimates (
2.78
)–(
2.79
), we obtain inequality (
2.70
).
∎

Now we are ready to show the following proposition.

Proposition 2.3
.

Under assumptions
\mathbf{(U_{0})}
and
\mathbf{(g)}
,
for a given fixed hypersonic similarity parameter
a_{\infty}
,
there exist constants
\varepsilon_{0}<\min\{\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}\})
and
\tau_{0}<\min\{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\}

depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
such that, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
and
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
,
if
U_{0}
and
g(x)
satisfy (
2.46
), then
the approximate solution
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
converges to
U^{(\tau)}_{h}
in
L^{1}(-\infty,g_{h}(x))
as
\nu\rightarrow\infty
.
Moreover, there exist a constant
L>0
, a domain
\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
, and a map
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0}):\mathcal{D}_{h,x^{\prime}_{0}}\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{h,x}\qquad\mbox{for all $x\geq x^{\prime}_{0}\geq 0$},

(2.80)

such that

(i)

For any
x\geq 0
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,\cdot)\in\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
,
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x)(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,\cdot))=U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,\cdot)
, and,
for all
x\geq x^{\prime}\geq x^{\prime}_{0}\geq 0
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\in\mathcal{D}_{h,x^{\prime}_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0}))=\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime})\circ\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}_{0})(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0}));

(2.81)

(ii)

If
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{D}_{h^{\prime},x}
are the map and domain corresponding
to the boundary function
g_{h^{\prime}}(x)
and initial data
V^{\nu}_{0}
, then, for any
x^{\prime\prime}\geq x^{\prime}>x^{\prime}_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}_{0})(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime}_{0})(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x^{\prime\prime})))}

\displaystyle\leq L\Big(|x^{\prime}-x^{\prime\prime}|+\|U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0})))}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,+\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((x_{0}^{\prime},\infty))}\Big),

(2.82)

where constant
L
depends only on
U_{\infty}
and
a_{\infty}
, and
we have used that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{h}^{(\tau)}(x^{\prime},x_{0})(U_{h}^{(\tau)}(x_{0},\cdot))=U_{h}^{(\tau)}(x^{\prime},\cdot)=\underline{U}\qquad\,\,\mbox{on $(g_{h}(x^{\prime}),\infty)$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{h^{\prime}}^{(\tau)}({x}^{\prime\prime},{x}_{0})(V_{h^{\prime}}^{(\tau)}({x}_{0},\cdot))=V_{h^{\prime}}^{(\tau)}(x^{\prime\prime},\cdot)=\underline{U}\qquad\mbox{on $(g_{h^{\prime}}(x^{\prime\prime}),\infty)$}.

Proof.

For two approximate solutions
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}=(\rho^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu},p^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})^{\top}

and
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}=(\hat{\rho}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu},\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu},\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu},\hat{p}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu})^{\top}
corresponding
to
(U^{\nu}_{0},g_{h})
and
(V^{\nu}_{0},g_{h^{\prime}})
, respectively,
it follows from estimates (
2.5
) and (
2.70
) that

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\check{g}_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\check{g}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0})))}

\displaystyle\quad+C\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}+C(x-x^{\prime}_{0})\nu^{-1}.

Notice that, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-g^{\prime}_{h}(s)\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}+\big\|g^{\prime}_{h}(s)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(s)\big\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg\|\Big(\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(s)\Bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}.

(2.83)

For the first term on the right side of (
2.5
), by Proposition
2.2
,
we can obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-g^{\prime}_{h}(s)\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}\Big)(s,g_{h}(s))\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|g_{h}(s)-g_{h^{\prime}}(s)\big\|_{L^{\infty}((x^{\prime}_{0},x))}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}+|g^{\prime}_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})|\Big),

where constant
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
, provided that
\tau
is sufficiently small.

In the same way, we can also apply Proposition
2.2

for
\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big(\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(s)\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x_{0}^{\prime},x))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|g_{h}(s)-g_{h^{\prime}}(s)\big\|_{L^{\infty}((x_{0}^{\prime},x))}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}+|g^{\prime}_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})|\Big),

provided that
\tau
is sufficiently small.

Inserting the above two estimates into (
2.5
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big(\frac{v^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}}-\frac{\hat{v}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}{1+\tau^{2}\hat{u}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}}\Big)(s,\check{g}_{h}(s))\bigg\|_{L^{1}((x_{0}^{\prime},x))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}+|g_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})|\big).

Therefore, there exists a constant
C_{2,10}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
such that

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\check{g}_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,10}\Big(\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\check{g}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0})))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\,+\big\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}+\big|g_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big|\Big)+C(x-x^{\prime}_{0})\nu^{-1}.\quad

We notice from Proposition
2.1
that

\displaystyle\big|g_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{\hat{g}_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})}_{\check{g}_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})}\big|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x_{0}^{\prime},s)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x_{0}^{\prime},s)\big|\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0})))}.

Then

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\check{g}_{h}(x)))}+\int^{\hat{g}_{h}(x)}_{\check{g}_{h}(x)}\big|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,s)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x,s)\big|\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0})))}+\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big|g_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})\big|+C(x-x^{\prime}_{0})\nu^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,11}\Big(\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0})))}+\big\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(x-x^{\prime}_{0})\nu^{-1},

(2.84)

where constant
C_{2,11}>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Now, let
x_{0}^{\prime}=0
,
h=h^{\prime}
, and
V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}=U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu^{\prime}}
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}-U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu^{\prime}}\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,9}\big\|U^{\nu}_{0}-U^{\nu^{\prime}}_{0}\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{I})}+C\,x\max\big\{\nu^{-1},\nu^{\prime-1}\big\}.

(2.85)

For given
U_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{h,0}
, we approximate
U_{0}
by
U^{\nu}_{0}
with property (
2.34
)
and construct the approximate solution
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
with boundary
y=g_{h}(x)
.
Thus,
\big\{U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}\big\}_{\nu>0}
is a Cauchy sequence and
converges in
L^{1}(-\infty,g_{h}(x))
to a unique limit
U^{(\tau)}_{h}
as
\nu\rightarrow\infty
.
Then define

\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(y))\doteq U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,y)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{h,x}\doteq\mathbf{cl}\left\{U^{(\tau)}_{h}-\underline{U}\in(L^{1}\cap BV)(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{4})\,:\begin{array}[]{l}U^{(\tau)}_{h}=\underline{U}\ \mbox{for $y>g_{h}(x)$},\\
\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})<\varepsilon\end{array}\right\},

where
\mathbf{cl}
denotes the closure in the
L^{1}
–topology, and
\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})=\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})+\mathcal{K}^{(\tau)}\mathbf{Q}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})=\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})+\sum^{4}_{i=2}\mathcal{K}^{(\tau)}_{i}\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}_{i}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})+\mathcal{K}^{(\tau)}_{c}\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}_{c}(U^{(\tau)}_{h}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}_{i}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})=\sum_{\alpha_{i}\in\mathcal{J}^{(\tau)}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{i}}|\qquad\,\mbox{for $1\leq i\leq 4$},

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{(\tau)}_{c}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})=\sum_{k>[\frac{x}{h}]}|\omega_{k}|,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h})=\sum_{(\alpha_{i},\beta_{j})\in\mathcal{A}^{(\tau)}\setminus\mathcal{NP}^{(\tau)}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\alpha_{i}}||\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}|.

By assumption (
2.46
), we notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{(\tau)}(0)<\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}(U^{\nu}_{0})<\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}(U_{0})<\varepsilon.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})<\mathcal{G}^{(\tau)}(x)<\mathcal{G}^{(\tau)}(0)<\varepsilon.

By the lower semicontinuity of
\mathbf{G}^{(\tau)}(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu})

and the convergence of
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
as
\nu\rightarrow\infty
,
we see that
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(y))\in\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
.

From the construction, we know that map
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
can start at any “time”
x\geq 0
.
Clearly,
for
x\geq 0
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
,

\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x)(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,y))=U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,y)

and, for any
x\geq x^{\prime}_{0}\geq 0
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\in\mathcal{D}_{h,x_{0}^{\prime}}
,

\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot))\in\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
.

To show equality (
2.81
) for map
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
, for a given solution
U^{(\tau)}_{h}
,
consider its approximate solutions
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu^{\prime}}(x,\cdot)
corresponding
to the initial data
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime},\cdot)
respectively for
\nu^{\prime}>\nu
.
By (
2.5
), we see that, for
x>x^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime})(U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x^{\prime},\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle=\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu^{\prime}}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq C(x-x^{\prime})\nu^{\prime-1},

where constant
C
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.
Taking the limit:
\nu
,
\nu^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty
above, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,\cdot)-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime})(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime},\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}=0,

which implies (
2.81
).

Finally, we show (
(ii)
).
For the Lipschtiz continuity of map
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})

with respect to
x
, it is a direct consequence of Proposition
2.1
.
For the Lipschitz continuity of
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})
with respect to the initial data,
by
(
2.5
),

\displaystyle\big\|U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2,11}\Big(\|U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)-V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}_{0},\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\check{g}_{h}(x_{0})))}+\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}((x^{\prime}_{0},\infty))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+|g_{h}(x_{0}^{\prime})-g_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0}^{\prime})|\Big).

Then estimate (
(ii)
) can be obtained by the triangle inequality,
and the Lipschitz continuity of
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})
with respect to both
x
and the initial data.
This completes the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
1.1
.

Proof of
Theorem
1.1
.

Consider two approximate solutions
U^{(\tau)}_{h}
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}
, which are the limits of
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
U^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime},\nu}

as
\nu\rightarrow\infty
respectively, to the same initial data
U_{0}
and the boundary functions
g_{h}
and
g_{h^{\prime}}
respectively.
Then, by (
(ii)
),

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,\hat{g}_{h}(x)))}\leq L\big\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}.

Note that the right-hand side of the above inequality tends to zero as
h
,
h^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
.
Thus,

\{\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\}_{h}
is a Cauchy sequence and
converges to a unique limit
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}(x,0)(U_{0})
,
i.e.
,

\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}(x,0)(U_{0})
in
L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))
as
h\rightarrow 0
.
Define

\mathcal{D}_{x}=\bigcap_{h>0}\mathcal{D}_{h,x}\subseteq\big\{U^{(\tau)}-\underline{U}\in(L^{1}\cap BV)(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{4})\,:\,U^{(\tau)}=\underline{U}\ \mbox{for}\ y>g(x)\big\}

(2.89)

and
U^{(\tau)}(x,y)\doteq\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}(x,0)(U_{0}(y))
.
Then, by Proposition
2.3
and the uniqueness of
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}
,
we know that
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}(x,x^{\prime}_{0}):[0,\infty)\times\mathcal{D}_{x_{0}}\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{x}
, and property (i) in Theorem
1.1
holds.
Furthermore, by the same arguments as done in
[
9
]
, using Proposition
2.1
and the Lebesgue dominate convergence theorem,
we can show that
U^{(\tau)}(x,y)
is a global entropy solution of Problem I.
Finally, consider the approximate solutions
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},y))

and
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime}_{0})(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x^{\prime}_{0},y))

corresponding to the initial states and the boundary functions
(U^{(\tau)}_{h}(x^{\prime}_{0},y),g_{h})
and
(V^{(\tau)}_{h^{\prime}}(x_{0},y),g_{h^{\prime}})
, respectively.
Then, by letting
h\rightarrow 0
and
h^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
, it follows from estimate (
(ii)
) that estimate (
(iii)
) holds.
∎

Finally, we remark that, by a similar argument, the well-posedness theorems for the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)
for
\tau=0
can also be obtained. We provide brief proofs of the well-posedness theorems in Appendix A.

3
Comparison between the Riemann Solvers for the Two Systems

In this section, we compare the Riemann solvers for system (
1.9
) and system (
1.12
) with appropriate initial and boundary conditions.

3.1
Comparison of the Riemann solvers away from the boundary

For the approximate solutions
U_{h,\nu}
constructed in Appendix A for system (
1.12
),
let
(\xi,y_{I})
be a discontinuity point of
U_{h,\nu}
. Define

\begin{split}&U_{a}\doteq(\rho_{a},u_{a},v_{a},p_{a})^{\top}=\lim_{y\rightarrow y_{I}+}U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y),\\
&U_{b}\doteq(\rho_{b},u_{b},v_{b},p_{b})^{\top}=\lim_{y\rightarrow y_{I}-}U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y).\end{split}

(3.1)

Let
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
be the Lipschtiz continuous map as given in Proposition
2.3
for system (
1.9
).
We first establish the following lemma.

Lemma 3.1
.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\})
,
let
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\underline{U})
be a given constant state, and let
|\sigma_{j}|<\varepsilon
be parameters for
1\leq j\leq 4
.
Then,
for
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
, and for maps

\Phi^{(\tau)}_{j}
and
\Phi_{j}
given by
Lemma
2.2
and

Lemma A.1

respectively,

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}_{j}(\sigma_{j};U_{b},\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=\Phi_{j}(\sigma_{j};U_{b})\qquad\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$},

(3.2)

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma};U_{b},\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=\Phi(\boldsymbol{\sigma};U_{b}),\quad\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}),\quad

(3.3)

where
\Phi(\boldsymbol{\sigma};U_{b})=\Phi_{4}(\sigma_{4};\Phi_{3}(\sigma_{3};\Phi_{2}(\sigma_{2};\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{1};U_{b}))))
.

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}_{j}(\sigma_{j};U_{b},\tau^{2})\Big|_{\sigma_{j}=0}=\Phi_{j}(\sigma_{j};U_{b})\Big|_{\sigma_{j}=0}=U_{b}\qquad\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

Using (
A.6
),
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Phi^{(\tau)}_{j}(\sigma_{j};U_{b},\tau^{2})}{\partial\sigma_{j}}\bigg|_{\sigma_{j}=0,\tau=0}=\frac{\partial\Phi_{j}(\sigma_{j};U_{b})}{\partial\sigma_{j}}\bigg|_{\sigma_{j}=0}=\boldsymbol{r}_{j}(U_{b})\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

Thus, by the uniqueness of solutions of the ordinary differential equations,
we can obtain (
3.2
) for
|\sigma_{j}|<\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}

and
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
.
Moreover, by (
2.1
) and (
3.2
), we conclude (
3.3
).
∎

We now consider the comparison of the Riemann solvers of problem (
2.14
) and problem (
A.7
)
with the same
below
and
above
states away from the boundary.

Lemma 3.2
.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\})
,
let
U_{a}
,
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\underline{U})
be two constant states defined by (
3.1
).
Let
\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}
for some
k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and
\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}\in\mathcal{NP}

be fronts with strength
\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}
and
\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}
, respectively, satisfying

\displaystyle U_{a}=\Phi_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}};U_{b})\qquad\mbox{or}\qquad\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}=|U_{a}-U_{b}|.

(3.4)

Let
\boldsymbol{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4})
be the physical wave-fronts with strengths

\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}})
and satisfy

\displaystyle U_{a}=\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2}).

(3.5)

Then, for the given fixed constant
a_{\infty}

and for
\tau<\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\}
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=\delta_{jk}\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|\tau^{2}\qquad\ \mbox{for $1\leq j,k\leq 4$},

(3.6)

or

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=O(1)\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}\qquad\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$},

(3.7)

where
\delta_{jk}
is the Kronecker symbol and the bound of
O(1)
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
,
but independent of
(\tau,h,\nu)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2
, we know that, for
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,

\displaystyle\det\bigg(\frac{\partial\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})}{\partial\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\bigg)\bigg|_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\boldsymbol{0},\ U_{b}=\underline{U}}\neq 0.

For
\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}
, using conditions (
3.4
)–(
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})=\Phi_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}};U_{b}),\qquad\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}).

Thus, it follows from the implicit function theorem that the above equations admit a unique solution

\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}},\tau^{2})

for
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
and
|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|<\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}
.

To estimate
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}},\tau^{2})
for
1\leq j\leq 4
, by Lemma
3.1
,

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\big|_{\tau=0};U_{b},\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=\Phi(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|_{\tau=0};U_{b})=\Phi_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}};U_{b}).

Thus, it follows from
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(0,\tau^{2})=0
and
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(0,\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=0
that

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}},\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=\delta_{jk}\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}.

Then, by the Taylor formula, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}},\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}+\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(0,\tau^{2})-\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(0,\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|\tau^{2}

\displaystyle=\delta_{jk}\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|\tau^{2},

which is exactly
estimate (
3.6
).

Next,
for
\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}\in\mathcal{NP}
, using estimate (
2.36
) in Lemma
2.2
and (
3.4
) for
\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}
,
we can follow the same argument above to obtain estimate (
3.7
).
∎

Based on Lemma
3.2
, we now consider the error estimates between the trajectory of
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))

and
U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)
away from the boundary for
s>0
sufficiently small.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
be the Lipschtiz continuous map as given in

for

.

,

U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)=\begin{cases}U_{a}\quad&\mbox{for $y>y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}s$},\\[2.0pt]
U_{b}\quad&\mbox{for $y<y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}s$},\end{cases}

(3.8)

where

For the fixed given hypersonic similarity parameter

is sufficiently small,

(i)

(3.10)

(3.12)

where all the constants

,

, depend only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

, since the case:

can be dealt with in the same way.
From Proposition
2.3
, we know that

\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{b}),\qquad\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}).

(3.13)

Then, by Lemma
3.2
, we have estimate (

) for
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}
with
1\leq j\leq 4
and
k=1
.
Then it follows from
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}<0
that
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}<0
,
if
\tau
is sufficiently small. This implies that
\beta_{1}\in\mathcal{S}^{(\tau)}_{1}
.

s
(\xi,y_{I})
x=\xi
x=\xi+s
y=y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s
\beta_{4}
\beta_{2(3)}
\alpha_{1}
\beta_{1}
y=y_{I}-\hat{\lambda}s
U_{a}
U^{(\tau)}_{m_{2(3)}}
U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}}
U_{b}

Fig. 3.1:
Comparison of the Riemann solutions for the case:
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}

We denote
U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}}
,
U^{(\tau)}_{m_{2}}
, and
U^{(\tau)}_{m_{3}}
as the intermediate states of the Riemann
solutions
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))
with wave strength
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
.
As shown in Fig.
3.1
, let
\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})
and
\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})

be the speeds of the shock-fronts
\alpha_{1}
and
\beta_{1}
, respectively.
From Lemma
2.2
and the results in

[
38
, Chapter 17]
, we have

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})\Big|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0}=\lambda_{1}(U_{b}),\quad\,\frac{\partial\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}\bigg|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0}=\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{U}\lambda_{1}(U_{b})\cdot\boldsymbol{r}_{1}(U_{b})=1,

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})\Big|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=0}=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2}),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})}{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}\bigg|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=0}=\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{U^{(\tau)}}\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})\cdot\boldsymbol{r}^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})=1.

Thus, using (
A.6
) and Lemma
3.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})

\displaystyle=\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})\Big|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=0}+\frac{\partial\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})}{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}\bigg|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=0}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\ -\bigg(\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})\Big|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0}+\frac{\partial\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}\bigg|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{b})+\frac{1}{2}\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}-\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\big)+O(1)\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}-\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\big)^{2}+O(1)\tau^{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=O(1)\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)\tau^{2}.

(3.14)

Now we begin to derive estimate (
3.9
). A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{y_{I}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{\min\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s\}}_{y_{I}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\tilde{x})(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\ +\int^{\max\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s\}}_{\min\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\ +\int^{y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{\max\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\doteq I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},1}+I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}+I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}.

(3.15)

It is clear that
I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},1}=0
.
For
I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}
, by Lemmas
2.2
and
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big|\big|U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}}-U_{b}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}(\sigma_{\alpha})|+2^{-\nu}\big)\big|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big((1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)\big(1+C\,\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}s,

or

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big|\big|U^{(\tau)}_{a}-U_{b}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big((1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}s.

For
I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}
, by Lemma
3.1
again, for both cases:
\beta_{4}\in\mathcal{S}^{(\tau)}_{4}
and
\beta_{4}\in\mathcal{R}^{(\tau)}_{4}
,
we have

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\sum_{j=1,2}\big|U^{(\tau)}_{m_{j}}-U^{(\tau)}_{m_{j+1}}\big|+\big|U_{a}-U^{(\tau)}_{m_{3}}\big|\bigg)s

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\sum^{3}_{j=2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}|\bigg)s

\displaystyle\leq C|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}s.

Finally, combining the estimates of
I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},1}
,
I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}
, and
I_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}
together,
we can choose a constant
C_{3,1}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
such that, for
s>0
sufficiently small,
estimate (
3.10
) holds.

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(U_{a})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=0.

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\sum^{4}_{j=2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}|\bigg)s

\displaystyle\leq C|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}s.

Finally, for the second term, we have

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\doteq\int^{\max\{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s\}}_{\min\{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

by three subcases based on the different location of
\alpha_{1}
.

s

\displaystyle=\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\,+\int^{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

For the first term, by (

)–(

)
together with Proposition A.1
and Lemma

, we obtain

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\leq\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{b},\tau^{2})\big|\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{b},\tau^{2})-U_{b}\big|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{b},\tau^{2})\big||\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{0}|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}\big)\big|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\big|s.

For the second term, using Lemma
3.2
and (
3.19
), we have

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\leq C\Big(\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{a})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu})|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

Combining the two estimates above, we conclude

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

(\xi,y_{I})

\beta_{4}
\beta_{2(3)}
\alpha_{1}
\beta_{1}

U_{a}

Fig. 3.3:
Subcase 2:

and
