Subcase 2
:
\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})<\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}<\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})
.
As shown in Fig.
3.3
, we can decompose
I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}
into two terms:

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}

\displaystyle=\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}})s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s}_{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

For the first term, it follows from (
3.6
), (
3.18
), (
3.20
), and
Proposition A.1
that

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\leq\big(\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})\big)\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{b},\tau^{2})-U_{b}\big|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\Big(\big|\lambda_{1}(U_{a})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

For the second term, further together with (
3.19
), we have

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})s}_{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\leq\big(\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big)\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{b},\tau^{2})-U_{a}\big|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\Big(\big|\lambda_{1}(U_{a})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\,\,\,\times\Big(\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{b},\tau^{2})-U_{b}\big|+|U_{a}-U_{b}|\Big)s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big((1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}

\displaystyle=\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}})s}_{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b})s}_{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}})s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

Then it follows from Lemma
3.2
,
Proposition A.1,
and (
3.20
) that

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b})s}_{y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big||U_{a}-U_{b}|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\Big(|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{a})|+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s,

and

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}})s}_{y_{I}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{b},\tau^{2})\big|\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2})-U_{a}\big|s

\displaystyle\,\leq C|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)s

\displaystyle\,\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

Thus, we also obtain in this subcase that

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

Now, for all the three subcases, we know that

I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

Then, combining the estimates for
I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},1}
,
I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}
, and
I_{\mathcal{R}_{1},3}
together,
we can choose a constant
C_{4,2}
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
so that (
3.10
) holds.

3. We know that
\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{C}_{k}
for
k=2,3
. Without loss of generality,

k=2
. In this case, we know that the solutions,
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})=\Phi_{2}(\sigma_{\alpha_{2}};U_{b})\qquad\mbox{for $\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}})$}.

By Lemma
3.2
, there exists a unique solution
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}})
of the above equation, which satisfies the estimates:

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}}=\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\tau^{2},\qquad\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\tau^{2}\quad\mbox{for $j\neq 2$}.

(3.21)

s
(\xi,y_{I})

Denoted by
U^{(\tau)}_{j},j=1,2,3
, the corresponding intermediate states of the Riemann solutions to (
2.15
) (see Fig.
3.5
).
Note that speeds
\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(\sigma_{\alpha_{2}})
and
\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}})
of fronts
\alpha_{2}
and
\beta_{2}
satisfy that
\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(\sigma_{\alpha_{2}})=\lambda_{2}(U_{b})
and

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(U_{b},\tau^{2})+O(1)|U^{(\tau)}_{m_{1}}-U_{b}|

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(U_{b},\tau^{2})+O(1)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(U_{b},\tau^{2})+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}.

Thus, using (
A.6
),
we have

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(\sigma_{\alpha_{2}})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(U_{b},\tau^{2})+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\tau^{2}-\lambda_{2}(U_{b})

\displaystyle=O(1)\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\big)\tau^{2}.

Now, notice that

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{y_{I}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\tilde{x})(U_{h,\nu}(\tilde{x},y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{\min\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\,\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}s\}}_{y_{I}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\ +\int^{\max\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\,\tau^{2})s,\tilde{y}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}s\}}_{\min\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\,\tau^{2})s,\,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\ +\int^{y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{\max\{y_{I}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\,\tau^{2})s,y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\doteq I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},1}+I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},2}+I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},3}.

It is clear that
I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},1}=0
.

For
I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},2}
, if
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}\leq\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})
, then

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}\big||U^{(\tau)}_{m_{2}}-U_{b}|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(\sigma_{\alpha_{2}})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1,2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}|\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\big)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\Big)\big(1+C\,\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|s;

otherwise, if
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}\geq\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})
, then

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{2}}-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})\big||U^{(\tau)}_{m_{2}}-U_{a}|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{2}(\sigma_{\alpha_{2}})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)\Big(\sum_{k=3,4}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{k}}|\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\big)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\tau^{2}s

\displaystyle\leq C|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\tau^{2}s.

This implies that

I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},2}\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|s.

For
I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},3}
, it follows from estimates (
3.21
) that

\displaystyle I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},3}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|U^{(\tau)}_{m_{2}}-U_{m_{3}}|+|U^{(\tau)}_{m_{3}}-U_{a}|\big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}}|+|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}|\big)s

\displaystyle\leq C|\sigma_{\alpha_{2}}|\tau^{2}s.

Therefore, we collect the estimates for
I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},1}
,
I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},2}
, and
I_{\mathcal{C}_{2},3}
altogether
to conclude that there exists a constant
C_{3,3}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})

such that estimate (
3.11
) holds.

Next, based on Proposition
3.1
, we consider the more general case when the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)

contains more than one wave.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
be the Lipschitz continuous map as given in
Proposition
2.3
for
Problem I
.
Let
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y)
be the approximate solution constructed in
Appendix A
for system (
1.12
)
with
U_{a}
,
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
defined by (
3.1
).
Assume that, near point
(\xi,y_{I})
,
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y)
is of the form
:

U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}U_{a},&y>y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s,\\[2.0pt]
\hat{U}_{a},&y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{\ell}}s<y<y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s,\\[2.0pt]
U_{m},&y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}s<y<y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{\ell}}s,\\[2.0pt]
U_{b},&y<y_{I}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}s,\end{array}\right.

(3.23)

where
\hat{\lambda}>\max_{1\leq j\leq 4}\big\{\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(U^{(\tau)},\tau^{2}),\lambda_{j}(U)\big\}
for
U^{(\tau)}
,

U\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
,

\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,
and
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}|
,
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{\ell}}|<\hat{\lambda}
for
k<\ell
with
1\leq k,\ell\leq 4
.
That is,
U_{b}
and
U_{m}
are connected by a
k^{\rm th}
wave that can be a shock
\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}

with
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}})|<2^{-\nu}
,
rarefaction-front
\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{R}_{k}
with
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}-\lambda_{k}(U_{m})|<2^{-\nu}
,
or vortex sheet/entropy wave
\alpha_{k}\in\mathcal{C}_{k}
with
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{k}}-\lambda_{k}(U_{b})|<2^{-\nu}
;

U_{m}
and
\hat{U}_{a}
are connected by an
l^{\rm th}
wave that could be shock
\alpha_{\ell}\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}

with
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{\ell}}-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{\ell}(\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}})|<2^{-\nu}
,
rarefaction-front
\alpha_{\ell}\in\mathcal{R}_{\ell}
with
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{\ell}}-\lambda_{\ell}(\hat{U}_{a})|<2^{-\nu}
,
or vortex sheet/entropy wave
\alpha_{\ell}\in\mathcal{C}_{\ell}
with
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{\ell}}-\lambda_{\ell}(U_{m})|<2^{-\nu}
;

and
\hat{U}_{a}
and
U_{a}
are connected by a non-physical wave
\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}\in\mathcal{NP}
,
where
\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}})
,
\lambda_{k}(U_{m})
, and
\lambda_{k}(U_{b})
are the speeds of
k^{\rm th}
shock-front,
rarefaction-front, and vortex sheet/entropy wave-front, respectively, for
1\leq k\leq 4
.
Then, for
s>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\int^{y_{I}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{y_{I}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\,\,\leq{C_{3,5}}\Big(\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}|\big)+\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}\Big)s,

(3.24)

where constant
C_{3,5}>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.3
, solution
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))
satisfies

U_{a}=\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}};U_{b},\tau^{2}),\,\hat{U}_{a}=\Phi_{\ell}(\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}};U_{m}),\,U_{m}=\Phi_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}};U_{b}),\,\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}=|U_{a}-\hat{U}_{a}|,

(3.25)

where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{4}})
.
Define

\Phi^{(\tau)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}};U_{b},\tau^{2})=\Phi_{\ell}(\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}};\Phi_{k}(\sigma_{\alpha_{k}};U_{b}))\quad\,\,\mbox{for $\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}=(\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{1}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{2}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{3}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{4}})$}.

(3.26)

By Lemma
2.3
, the implicit function theorem, and (
3.25
)–(
3.26
),
for
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
and
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{j}}=\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{j}}+O(1)\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}\qquad\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

(3.27)

Thus, to estimate
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{j}}
for
1\leq j\leq 4
for the completion of the proof of estimate (
3.2
),
one needs to estimate
\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{j}}
for
1\leq j\leq 4
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}=(\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{1}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{2}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{3}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{4}})

and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime\prime}}=(\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{1}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{2}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{3}},\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{4}})

satisfy

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{a}=\Phi^{(\tau)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime\prime}};U_{m},\tau^{2}),\qquad\hat{U}_{m}=\Phi^{(\tau)}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}};U_{b},\tau^{2})

(3.28)

Then, by (
3.25
) and Lemma
3.2
, we have

\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}=\delta_{jk}\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|\tau^{2},\quad\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}=\delta_{j\ell}\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}|\tau^{2}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

(3.29)

By (
3.26
), (
3.28
), and Lemma
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}_{j}}=\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}+\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}+O(1)\Delta\big(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime\prime}}\big),

(3.30)

where

\Delta\big(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime\prime}}\big)=\Big(\sum^{4}_{j=2}|\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}|\Big)|\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}|+\Big(\sum^{3}_{j=2}|\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}|\Big)|\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{4}}|+\sum^{4}_{j=1}\Delta_{j}\big(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime\prime}}\big),

and

\left.\Delta_{j}\right(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime\prime}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}0,&\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}>0\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}>0,\\[5.0pt]
|\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}||\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}|,&\hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}_{j}}<0\ \ \mbox{or}\ \ \hat{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}<0.\end{array}\right.

Therefore, it follows from (
3.27
) and (
3.29
)–(
3.30
) that, for
1\leq j\leq 4
and
1\leq k<\ell\leq 4
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\gamma_{j}}=\delta_{jk}\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}+\delta_{j\ell}\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}|\big)\tau^{2}+O(1)\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}.

(3.31)

With estimate (
3.31
) in hand, we can now follow the method exactly as done in the proof of Proposition
3.1

to deduce estimates (
3.2
). Since the proof is long but the same, we omit it.
∎

3.2
Comparison of the Riemann solvers with the boundary

We first study the case when all the Riemann solutions are generated by corner points on the approximate boundary
\Gamma_{h}

(see also Fig.
3.7
).
Let
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}
and
U_{h,\nu}
be approximate solutions of Problem I (obtained in Proposition
2.1
)
and the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
) (obtained in Proposition A.1),
respectively.
Let
\textsc{C}_{k}(x_{k},g_{k})
be the corner points on the approximate boundary
\Gamma_{h}
with turning angle
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}
.
Define

\displaystyle U_{B}=(\rho_{B},u_{B},v_{B},p_{B})^{\top}\doteq U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},g_{k}-),\,\,\,U_{\Gamma}=(\rho_{\Gamma},u_{\Gamma},v_{\Gamma},p_{\Gamma})^{\top}\doteq U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},g_{k}),

(3.32)

\displaystyle U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}=(\rho^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},u^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},v^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},p^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma})^{\top}\doteq U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},g_{k}).

(3.33)

\textsc{C}_{k-1}
\textsc{C}_{k}
\textsc{C}_{k+1}
\beta_{1}
\alpha_{1}
U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}
U_{\Gamma}
U_{B}
x_{k-1}
x_{k}
x_{k+1}

Fig. 3.7:
Comparison of the Riemann solutions near boundary
\Gamma_{h}

Then we establish the following lemma.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
U_{B},\,U_{\Gamma},\,U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})

be constant states defined by (
3.32
)–(
3.33
) and satisfy

\displaystyle U_{\Gamma}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}),\qquad U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}=\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2}),

(3.34)

\displaystyle v_{B}=\tan(\theta_{k}),\quad v_{\Gamma}=\tan(\theta_{k+1}),\quad(1+\tau^{2}u^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},v^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma})\cdot\mathbf{n}_{k+1}=0,\qquad\quad

(3.35)

where

\mathbf{n}_{k+1}=\big(\sin(\theta_{k+1}),-\cos(\theta_{k+1})\big)
.
For a given hypersonic similarity parameter
a_{\infty}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\})
, and
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,
when
|\theta_{k}|+|\omega_{k}|<\varepsilon
, then

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)\tau^{2}

(3.36)

\displaystyle=O(1)(1+\tau^{2})|\omega_{k}|,

(3.37)

where
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}
.

Proof.

By (
3.34
)–(
3.35
), we know that
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
and
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}

satisfy the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{B}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2};U_{B})

\displaystyle\doteq\Phi^{(3)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-\big(1+\tau^{2}\Phi^{(\tau),(2)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})\big)\Phi^{(3)}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})

\displaystyle=0.

(3.38)

By direct computation and using Remark
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}_{B}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2};U_{B})}{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}\Bigg|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0,\ U_{B}=\underline{U}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\Phi^{(3)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})}{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}\Bigg|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=0,\ U_{B}=\underline{U}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{r}^{(\tau),(3)}_{1}(\underline{U},\tau^{2})=e_{1}(\underline{U},\tau^{2})\neq 0

for
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
.
Then, by the implicit function theorem,
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}
can be solved uniquely from equation (
3.2
)
as a
C^{2}
–function of
(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})
.

Notice that, when
\tau=0
, by Lemma
3.1
and (
3.2
),

\displaystyle\Phi^{(3)}_{1}\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\tau=0};U_{B},\tau^{2}\big)\Big|_{\tau=0}=\Phi^{(3)}_{1}\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\tau=0};U_{B}\big)=\Phi^{(3)}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}),

so that

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\tau=0}=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\quad\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=\tau=0}=0,\quad\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0}=O(1)\tau^{2}.

Therefore, by applying the Taylor formula, it follows that

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\tau=0}+\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=0}-\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=\tau=0}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)\tau^{2},

which is estimate (
3.36
).

Next, by Lemma A.3,
for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, we know that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=K_{B}\omega_{k}

with
K_{B}>0
. Then, substituting it into (
3.36
),
we can derive estimate (
3.37
).
∎

Based on Lemma
3.3
, we have

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\textrm{C}_{k}(x_{k},g_{k})
with
g_{k}=g(x_{k})
be a corner point on the approximate boundary
\Gamma_{h}
.
Let
U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}
,
U_{\Gamma}
,
U_{B}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
.
For
s>0
,

Lemma A.3

implies that

U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)=\begin{cases}U_{\Gamma},\quad&g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s<y\leq g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s,\\[5.0pt]
U_{B},\quad&y<g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\end{cases}

(3.39)

where
U_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
,
and
v_{B}
and
v_{\Gamma}
satisfy (
3.35
) and
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}|<\hat{\lambda}
for a fixed constant
\hat{\lambda}

satisfying

\hat{\lambda}>\max_{1\leq j\leq 4}\big\{\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(U^{(\tau)},\tau^{2}),\lambda_{j}(U)\big\}

for
U^{(\tau)},U\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
.
Let
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
be the Lipschtiz map generated by
Problem I
obtained in
Proposition
2.3
.
Then, for a fixed given hypersonic similarity parameter
a_{\infty}
and for
s>0
sufficiently small, the following
statements hold
:

(i)

if
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}>0
, then
U_{B}
and
U_{\Gamma}
are connected by the
1^{\rm st}

rarefaction-front
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}
with strength
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}>0
and
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})|<2^{-\nu}

such that

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s}_{g_{k}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq{C_{3,6}}\Big(\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2}+\big(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2}\big)(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\Big)s,

(3.40)

where constant
C_{3,6}>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

(ii)

if
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}<0
, then
U_{B}
and
U_{\Gamma}
are connected by the
1^{\rm st}
shock-front
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}

with strength
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}<0
and
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})|<2^{-\nu}

such that

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s}_{g_{k}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq{C_{3,7}}\Big(\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2}+\big(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2}\big)2^{-\nu}\Big)s,

(3.41)

where
constant
C_{3,7}>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Case (i):
\omega_{k}>0
.
It follows from
Lemma A.3
that
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}>0
.
Then
U_{B}
and
U_{\Gamma}
are connected by the
1^{\rm st}
rarefaction-front.
Meanwhile, it follows from Proposition
2.3
that equation (
3.2
) admits
solution
P^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}(x_{k},y))
.
Moreover, for
s>0
sufficiently small, based on the construction of the Lipschitz continuous
map
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
, we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\Gamma)}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\ &\zeta\in\big[\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2}),\,\tan(\theta_{k+1})\big),\\[2.0pt]
\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{B},\tau^{2}),\ &\zeta\in\big[\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2}),\,\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})\big),\\[2.0pt]
U_{B},\ &\zeta\in(-\hat{\lambda},\,\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})),\end{cases}

(3.42)

where
\zeta=\frac{y-g_{k}}{s}
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2}))=0,\qquad\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2}))=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}.

(3.43)

Moreover, by Lemma
3.3
,
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}
satisfies estimate (
3.36
) and
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}>0
when
\tau
is sufficiently small.
Thus, it is also a rarefaction-front of the
1^{\rm st}
family.

Now, to derive estimate (
(i)
),
we rewrite its left-hand side as

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s}_{g_{k}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{\min\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\,g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s\}}_{g_{k}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\int^{\max\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\,g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s\}}_{\min\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\,g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\int^{g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s}_{\max\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\,g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\doteq C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},1}+C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}+C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},3}.

For
C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},1}
, it is clear that

C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},1}=0.

For
C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},3}
, by direct computation, we have

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},3}\leq C|U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}-U_{\Gamma}|s=C\big|\Phi_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})\big|s.

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\Gamma}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})\doteq\Phi_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}).

By Lemma
3.3
, we know that
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}
is a function
of
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
. Note that
\mathcal{E}_{\Gamma}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},0)=\mathcal{E}_{\Gamma}(0,0)=0
and
\mathcal{E}_{\Gamma}(0,\tau^{2})=O(1)\tau^{2}
.
Then, by the Taylor formula,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\Gamma}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})=O(1)\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)\tau^{2}=O(1)\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2},

(3.44)

so that

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},3}\leq C\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2}.

For
C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}
, by Lemma
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2}),\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}))

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{B})+O(1)\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}-\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle=O(1)(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|)\tau^{2}

\displaystyle=O(1)(1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2},\

(3.45)

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}(\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}))-\lambda_{1}(U_{B})+\lambda_{1}(U_{B})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=O(1)(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=O(1)\big(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2}\big),

(3.46)

where we have used (
A.6
)
for
U=U_{B}
.

\textsc{C}_{k-1}
\textsc{C}_{k}
\textsc{C}_{k+1}
\beta_{1}
\alpha_{1}
U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}
U_{\Gamma}
U_{B}
x_{k-1}
x_{k}
x_{k+1}

Fig. 3.8:
Case:
\omega_{k}>0
and
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}<\lambda^{{\tau}}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})

If
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}<\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})
(see Fig.
3.8
),
then
C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

First of all, using (
3.2
) and Lemma
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big||U_{\Gamma}-U_{B}|s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\Big(\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\omega_{k}|s.

Then, using the properties of the rarefaction wave
\beta_{1}
and (
3.44
)–(
3.2
), we can also obtain

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})\big|\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\,\Big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-U_{B}\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\,\times\Big(|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})|+|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})\nu^{-1}\big)s.

Therefore, it follows the three estimates above that

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\big)s.

Fig. 3.9:
Case:
\omega_{k}>0
and

If
\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})<\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\leq\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})
(see Fig.
3.9
), then

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

Then, by Lemma
3.2
,
Proposition A.1,
and estimates (
3.44
)–(
3.2
),

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\big|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})\big|\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{B},\tau^{2})-U_{B}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{\Gamma})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\big)s,

and

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\big(\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big)\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{B},\tau^{2})-U_{\Gamma}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\zeta);U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big((1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)\big(|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+(1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2}\big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\big)s.

Then

C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\big)s.

\textsc{C}_{k-1}
\textsc{C}_{k}
\textsc{C}_{k+1}

Fig. 3.10:

and

If

(see Fig.
3.10
), then

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{R}_{2},3}

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

Using estimate (
3.2
), Lemma
3.2
, and
Proposition A.1,
we have

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U_{B},\tau^{2})\big|\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-U_{B}\big|s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\,|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|^{2}s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+|\omega_{k}|\big)\nu^{-1}s,

and

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{g_{k}+\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\quad\leq\big|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})\big|\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-U_{B}\big|s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big(|\lambda^{(\tau)}_{1}(U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma},\tau^{2})-\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})|+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big((1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)(1+\tau^{2})|\omega_{k}|s

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\big)s.

Therefore, we obtain

C_{\mathcal{R}_{1},2}\leq C\big(|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}+(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\big)s.

Then
we can choose a constant
C_{3,6}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
so that estimate (
(i)
) holds.

First, we have

\displaystyle\int^{g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s}_{g_{k}-\hat{\lambda}h}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\int^{\max\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\,g_{k}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s\}}_{\min\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,g_{k}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\int^{g_{k}+\tan(\theta_{k+1})s}_{\max\{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\,g_{k}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s\}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\doteq C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},1}+C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}+C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}.

It is clear that

C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},1}=0.

For
C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}
, by Lemma
3.2
, we have

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})=O(1)\big(1+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\big)\tau^{2}=O(1)\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2}.

(3.47)

If
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}>\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})
, then, by (
3.47
) and Lemma
3.2
,

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}_{g_{k}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\,\big|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})\big|\big|U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}-U_{B}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)\big|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big((1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\Big)\big(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2}\big)s.

If
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}<\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})
,
then it follows from (
3.37
) and (
3.47
) that

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}

\displaystyle=\int^{g_{k}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})s}_{g_{k}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U^{(\tau),\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k},y))-U^{\Gamma}_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\,\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})-\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}\big|\big|U_{\Gamma}-U_{B}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})\big|+2^{-\nu}\Big)\big|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big((1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\Big)|\omega_{k}|s.

Therefore, we obtain

C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},2}\leq C\Big((1+|\omega_{k}|)\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2})\Big)s.

For
C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}
, it is direct to see

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{S}_{1},3}

\displaystyle\leq C\,|U^{(\tau)}_{\Gamma}-U_{\Gamma}|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma_{\beta_{1}};U_{B},\tau^{2})-\Phi(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})\big|s

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2}s.

Then
we can obtain estimate (
3.39
) by choosing

C_{3,7}>0
depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.
∎

Finally, in this section, we consider the comparison of the Riemann solvers near the approximate boundary
\Gamma_{h}
but away from the corner points, as shown in Fig.
3.12
.
Let
U_{h,\nu}
be the approximate solution of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
).
Let
\alpha_{1}
be the front issuing from the corner point
\textsc{C}_{k}(x_{k},g_{k})
for
k\geq 1
.
Let
y_{\alpha_{1}}(x)
be the location of front
\alpha_{1}
with a fixed speed
\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}
, which satisfies
y_{\alpha_{1}}(x_{k})=g_{k}
.
We first establish the following lemma.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
U_{B}=(\rho_{B},u_{B},v_{B},p_{B})^{\top}
and
U_{\Gamma}=(\rho_{\Gamma},u_{\Gamma},v_{\Gamma},p_{\Gamma})^{\top}

be the two constant states as defined in (
3.32
) and satisfy

U_{\Gamma}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}),\,\,\,U_{\Gamma}=\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{B},\tau^{2}),\,\,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}\big),

(3.48)

and

\displaystyle v_{B}=\tan(\theta_{k}),\qquad v_{\Gamma}=\tan(\theta_{k+1}).

(3.49)

Then, for
U_{B},\ U_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})

and for
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\qquad\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=O(1)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}\quad\mbox{for $j\neq 1$}.

(3.50)

Furthermore, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\})
, if
|\theta_{k}|+|\omega_{k}|<\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=O(1)\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2},\qquad\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=O(1)|\omega_{k}|\tau^{2}\quad\mbox{for $j\neq 1$},

(3.51)

where
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}
, and the bound of
O(1)
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

By (
3.48
), we know that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}\big)
satisfies

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{B},\tau^{2})=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B}).

(3.52)

Then, by Lemma
2.3
and the implicit function theorem, for
U_{B}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})

and
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
, equation (
3.52
) admits a unique solution

\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})\in C^{2}
with
1\leq j\leq 4
.
Moreover, from equation (
3.52
) and Lemma
3.1
, we see that

\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},0)=\delta_{j1}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\quad\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(0,\tau^{2})=0.

Thus, estimates (
3.50
) follows from the Taylor formula.

Then, using the boundary conditions (
3.49
) and
Lemma A.3,
we further obtain that
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=O(1)\omega_{k}
,
which leads to estimate (
3.51
). This completes the proof.
∎

Let
(\xi,y_{\Gamma})
be a point on front
y_{\alpha_{1}}(x)
that lies on the lower side of the approximate boundary
\Gamma_{h}

(see Fig.
3.12
).
Then we have the following proposition:

Proposition 3.4
.

For a given hypersonic similarity parameter
a_{\infty}
and for
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,
let
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
be a uniformly Lipschtiz continuous map obtained in
Proposition
2.3
.
Let
U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi,y)
be the approximate solution of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)
with
U_{B},\ U_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
defined in (
3.32
)
and satisfying (
3.49
). By

Lemma A.3
,
for
s>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}U_{\Gamma},&y_{\Gamma}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s<y<g_{h}(\xi+s),\\[2.0pt]
U_{B},&y<y_{\Gamma}+\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}s,\end{array}\right.

where
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}|\leq\hat{\lambda}
and
\hat{\lambda}
is a fixed constant
satisfying

\hat{\lambda}>\max_{1\leq j\leq 4}\{\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(U^{(\tau)},\tau^{2}),\lambda_{j}(U)\}\qquad\,\,\mbox{{\rm for} $U^{(\tau)},U\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})$}.

Then

(i)

if
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}>0
,
U_{B}
and
U_{\Gamma}
are connected by the
1^{\rm st}
rarefaction-front
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{R}_{1}

satisfying
U_{\Gamma}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})
with
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}>0
and
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\lambda_{1}(U_{\Gamma})|<2^{-\nu}
,
and

\displaystyle\int^{y_{\Gamma}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{y_{\Gamma}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq{C_{3,8}}\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\omega_{k}|s.

(3.56)

(ii)

if
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}<0
,
U_{B}
and
U_{\Gamma}
are connected by the
1^{\rm st}
shock-front
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}

satisfying
U_{\Gamma}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})
with
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}<0
and
|\dot{y}_{\alpha_{1}}-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})|<2^{-\nu}
,
and

\displaystyle\int^{y_{\Gamma}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{y_{\Gamma}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq{C_{3,9}}\big(\tau^{2}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\omega_{k}|,

(3.57)

where
\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})
is the exact speed of the
1^{\rm st}
shock-front
\alpha_{1}
,
and constants
C_{3,8}>0
and
C_{3,9}>0
depend only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

Proof.

For case (i),
i.e.
,
\omega_{k}=\theta_{k+1}-\theta_{k}>0
, by
Lemma A.3
and conditions (
3.49
),
we know that
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=K_{B}\omega_{k}>0
for a constant

and satisfies
U_{\Gamma}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{B})
.
Moreover, note that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}\big)
can be solved from equation (
3.52
),

3.48
).

3.4
to show that
\sigma_{\beta_{j}}
,

, satisfy estimate (

).

that

\displaystyle\int^{y_{\Gamma}+\hat{\lambda}s}_{y_{\Gamma}-\hat{\lambda}s}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U^{B}_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|s.

Therefore, estimate (

) follows from the estimate on
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
as stated above.
The proof of estimate (

) is similar as the one for (
(i)
),
so we omit it.
∎

4
Proof of Theorem
1.2
for Problem II

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.2
by establishing the optimal convergence rate with respect to the
L^{1}
–norm
between the entropy solution
U^{(\tau)}
of Problem I and the entropy solution
U
of the initial-boundary value problem
(
1.11
)–(
1.13
).
We first show a lemma to the
L^{1}
–error formula for
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}
with boundary.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
U(x):[0,X]\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
be a piecewise Lipschitz continuous function for some
X>0
with a finite number of wave-fronts,
where domain
\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
is given by (
2.5
).
Let
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,x^{\prime}_{0}):\mathcal{D}_{h,x^{\prime}_{0}}\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{h,x}
be the Lipschitz continuous map
\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}

generated by
Problem I
corresponding to an approximate boundary
\Gamma_{h}
as given in
Proposition
2.3
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U(0))-U(x)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{L}\int^{x}_{0}\overline{\lim}_{s\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U(\xi))-U(\xi+s)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi+s)))}}{{s}}{\rm d}\xi,

(4.1)

where constant
\bar{L}
depends only on the Lipschitz constant
L
given in
Proposition
2.3
.

Proof.

Define the set

\displaystyle\Lambda

\displaystyle\doteq\bigg\{x\in[0,X]\,:\,\begin{array}[]{ll}\mbox{ there are at least two interacting wave-fronts in $U$,}\\[2.0pt]
\mbox{ or $\,$ a wave-front hitting boundary $y=g_{h}(x)$, $\,$ at $x$}\end{array}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\subset[0,X].

Let
|\Lambda|=\ell
, and let
a_{k}\in\Lambda
,
k=1,2,\cdots,\ell
,
satisfy
a_{1}<a_{2}<\cdot\cdot\cdot<a_{\ell}
. For any
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
, define

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{0}=0,\ \tilde{x}_{1}=a_{1}-\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2\ell},\ \tilde{x}_{2}=a_{1}+\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2\ell},\,\cdots,\,\tilde{x}_{2\ell-1}=a_{\ell}-\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2\ell},

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{2\ell}=a_{\ell}+\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2\ell},\ \tilde{x}_{2\ell+1}=X.

Then we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,0)(U(0))-U(X)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(X)))}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{2\ell+1}_{k=1}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{k-1}))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{k})(U(\tilde{x}_{k}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(X)))}

\displaystyle=\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k+1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k+1}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(X)))}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\sum^{\ell}_{k=1}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1}))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(X)))}

\displaystyle\doteq J_{1}+J_{2}.

For
J_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k+1})\circ\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\,\,\,-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k+1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k+1}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(X)))}

\displaystyle\leq L\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))-U(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})))}

\displaystyle=L\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\,\,-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k+1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k+1}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})))}.

We claim:

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k+1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k+1}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})))}

\displaystyle\leq L\int^{\tilde{x}_{2k+1}}_{\tilde{x}_{2k}}\overline{\lim}_{s\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U(\xi))-U(\xi+s)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi+s)))}}{{s}}\,{\rm d}\xi.

(4.2)

In fact,
consider a partition
\{\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}\}^{k}_{j=1}
of interval
[\tilde{x}_{2k},\tilde{x}_{2k+1}]
:

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{2k}\doteq\tilde{x}^{1}_{2k}<\tilde{x}^{2}_{2k}<\cdot\cdot\cdot<\tilde{x}^{k}_{2k}\doteq\tilde{x}_{2k+1}.

Let
\lambda=\max_{1\leq j\leq k-1}\{\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}-\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}_{2k+1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k+1}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})))}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{k-1}_{j=1}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})))}

\displaystyle\leq\sum^{k-1}_{j=1}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})\circ\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k},\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}))

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\,\,\,-P^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1},\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k+1})))}

\displaystyle\leq L\sum^{k-1}_{j=1}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k},\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}))-U(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})))}

\displaystyle\leq L\sum^{k}_{j=1}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k},\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}))-U(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k})))}}{\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}-\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k}}\big(\tilde{x}^{j}_{2k}-\tilde{x}^{j+1}_{2k}\big).

Taking
\lambda\rightarrow 0
, we can obtain claim (
4.2
) so that

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq L^{2}\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\int^{\tilde{x}_{2k+1}}_{\tilde{x}_{2k}}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U(\xi))-U(\xi+s)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi+s)))}}{{s}}\,{\rm d}\xi.

For
J_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k})\circ\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k},\tilde{x}_{2k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1}))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\,\,-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(X,\tilde{x}_{2k})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(X)))}

\displaystyle\leq L\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k},\tilde{x}_{2k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1}))-U(\tilde{x}_{2k})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k})))}

\displaystyle\leq L\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k},\tilde{x}_{2k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1}))-U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k})))}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +L\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1})-U(\tilde{x}_{2k})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k})))}

\displaystyle\doteq J_{21}+J_{22}.

First, for
J_{21}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{21}

\displaystyle=L\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k},\tilde{x}_{2k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1}))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\,\,-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k-1},\tilde{x}_{2k-1})(U(\tilde{x}_{2k-1}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\tilde{x}_{2k})))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}(\tilde{x}_{2k}-\tilde{x}_{2k-1})\leq C\ell\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2\ell}=C\tilde{\epsilon},

while, for
J_{22}
, it follows from the Lipschitz continuity of
U(x)
that

\displaystyle J_{22}\leq C\sum^{\ell}_{k=0}(\tilde{x}_{2k}-\tilde{x}_{2k-1})\leq C\tilde{\epsilon}.

Thus, with the estimates for
J_{21}
and
J_{22}
, we obtain that
J_{2}\leq C\tilde{\epsilon}
.
Finally, combining the estimates for
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
and letting
\tilde{\epsilon}\rightarrow 0
,
we conclude (
4.1
).
∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.2
, which gives a positive answer to Problem II.

Proof of
Theorem
1.2
.

We first show estimate (
1.26
).
This is achieved by three steps:

1.
Estimate on
\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi+s)))}
for sufficiently small
s
.
Let
U_{h,\nu}(x,y)
be the approximate solution of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)
corresponding to the approximate boundary
y=g_{h}(x)
and the approximate initial data
U^{\nu}_{0}(y)
with (
2.31
) and (
2.34
).
Let the jumps of
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y)
at
x=\xi>0
be
y_{1}>y_{2}>\cdots>y_{N}
with
y_{1}\leq g_{h}(\xi)
.
Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of indices
\alpha\in\{1,2,\cdot\cdot\cdot,N\}
such that
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y_{\alpha}+)
and
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y_{\alpha}-)

are connected by a shock-front with strength
\sigma_{\alpha}
.
Let
\mathcal{R}
(resp.,
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{NP}
) be the set of indices
\alpha\in\{1,2,\cdot\cdot\cdot,N\}

such that
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y_{\alpha}+)
and
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y_{\alpha}-)
are connected
by a rarefaction-front (resp., a vortex sheet/entropy wave and a non-physical wave) with strength
\sigma_{\alpha}
(or
\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}
for the non-physical waves), respectively.

If
\xi=[\frac{\xi}{h}]h
, then
\xi=x_{k}
for
k=[\frac{\xi}{h}]
.
According to the construction of the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
in Appendix A,
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y)
has no interaction on line
\xi=x_{k}

and no reflection at the corner point
\textsc{C}_{k}(x_{k},g_{k})
.
Thus, we choose
s>0
sufficiently small such that there is no interaction or reflection of the fronts for
U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y)
between
lines
\xi=x_{k}
and
\xi=x_{k}+s
.
Let
\eta=\frac{1}{2}\min_{1\leq j\leq N-1}\big\{b_{h}(\varsigma)-y_{1},y_{j}-y_{j+1}\big\}
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x_{k}+s)))}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{NP}}\int^{y_{\alpha}+\eta}_{y_{\alpha}-\eta}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,\cdot)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\int^{g_{h}(x_{k}+s)}_{g_{k}-\eta}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,\cdot)\big|\,{\rm d}y.

(4.3)

For
U_{h,\nu}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
and
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,
by Propositions
3.1
–
3.2
and
Proposition A.1,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{NP}}\int^{y_{\alpha}+\eta}_{y_{\alpha}-\eta}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,\cdot)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)\Big(\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}}|\sigma_{\alpha}|\Big)s+C\Big(\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{NP}}\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(\|U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot)\|_{BV((-\infty,g_{h}(x_{k})))}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)s.

By Proposition
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\int^{g_{h}(x_{k}+s)}_{g_{k}-\eta}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,\cdot)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(1+|\omega_{k}|\big)\tau^{2}+\big(|\omega_{k}|+\tau^{2}\big)(\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu})\Big)\Big(\sum_{k\geq 0}|\omega_{k}|\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\sum_{k\geq 0}|\omega_{k}|\Big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+1\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)s.

Then, combining the above two estimates altogether, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x_{k}+s,x_{k})(U_{h,\nu}(x_{k},\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x_{k}+s,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x_{k}+s)))}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+1\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)s,

(4.4)

where
C>0
depends only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
.

If
\xi>[\frac{\xi}{h}]h
, then
(\xi,g_{h}(\xi))
is not a corner point.
For
s>0
sufficiently small, and for
U_{h,\nu}\in\mathcal{O}_{\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}}(\underline{U})
and
\tau\in(0,\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\})
,
by Propositions
3.1
–
3.3
and
Proposition A.1,
we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi+s)))}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{NP}}\int^{y_{\alpha}+\hat{\eta}}_{y_{\alpha}-\hat{\eta}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,\cdot)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\,+\int^{g_{h}(\xi+s)}_{y_{1}-\hat{\eta}}\big|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,\cdot)\big|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)\Big(\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}}|\sigma_{\alpha}|\Big)s+O(1)\Big(\sum_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}\in\mathcal{NP}}\sigma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{NP}}}\Big)s

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\,+C\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)\Big(\sum_{k\geq 0}|\omega_{k}|\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(\|U_{h,\nu}(\xi,\cdot)\|_{((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi)))}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{I}}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)s,

(4.5)

where
k=[\frac{\xi}{h}]
and
\hat{\eta}=\frac{1}{2}\min_{1\leq j\leq N-1}\big\{y_{j}-y_{j+1}\big\}
.

2.
Estimate on
\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(y))-U_{h,\nu}(x,y)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}
.

From the construction of the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
in Appendix A,
we know that
U_{h,\nu}
satisfies the assumptions in Lemma
4.1
.
Then it follows from
Lemma
4.1
and estimates (
4.4
)–(
4
) that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(y))-U_{h,\nu}(x,y)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle=\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(y))-U_{h,\nu}(x,y)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{L}\int^{x}_{0}\overline{\lim}_{s\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(\xi+s,\xi)(U_{h,\nu}(\xi,y))-U_{h,\nu}(\xi+s,y)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(\xi+s)))}}{s}\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{L}\int^{x}_{0}\Big(\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+1\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{L}\Big(\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+h\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)x.

(4.6)

3.
Completion of the proof of estimate (
1.26
)
.
As shown in Proposition
2.3
and
Proposition A.2,
for any given
\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
h>0
, we can construct a global approximate solution
U^{(\tau)}_{h,\nu}

of Problem I and a global approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
of the initial boundary value
problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
), respectively,
corresponding to the initial data
U^{\nu}_{0}
and the approximate boundary
y=g_{h}(x)
.
Let
U^{(\tau)}
and
U
be the entropy solutions of Problem I and the initial boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
), respectively,
corresponding to the initial data
U_{0}
for boundary
y=g(x)
.
By Theorem
1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))=U^{(\tau)}(x,\cdot)\qquad\mbox{in $L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))$}

as
h\rightarrow 0
. By
Proposition A.2,

\displaystyle U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)\rightarrow U(x,\cdot)\qquad\mbox{in $L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\Omega)$ as $h\rightarrow 0$ and $\nu\rightarrow\infty$}.

Then,
by the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|U^{(\tau)}(x,\cdot)-U(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,b(x)))}\\[1.0pt]
&\leq\big\|U^{(\tau)}(x,\cdot)-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0})\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}\\[1.0pt]
&\quad+\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0})-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}\\[1.0pt]
&\quad+\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}\\[1.0pt]
&\quad+\big\|U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-U(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}.\end{split}

(4.7)

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
4.7
), by Propositions
2.1
–
2.3
and estimate (
2.34
),
we can choose constants
\varepsilon^{*}_{0}
and
\tau^{*}_{0}
sufficiently small, depending only on
(\underline{U},a_{\infty})
, such that,
for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon^{*})
, if
\tau\in(0,\tau^{*}_{0})
and
\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}<\varepsilon
, then

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((\min\{g_{h}(x),g(x)\},\,\max\{g_{h}(x),g(x)\}))}

\displaystyle\leq L\|U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot)-U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{I})}+\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\underline{U}\big\|_{L^{\infty}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}\big\|g_{h}(\cdot)-g(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))-\underline{U}\big\|_{L^{\infty}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}\big\|g_{h}(\cdot)-g(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|(\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\underline{U},\,\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))-\underline{U})\big\|_{L^{\infty}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}\big\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+L\|U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot)-U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{I})}.

It follows from (
2.31
) and (
2.34
) that, as
\nu\rightarrow\infty
and
h\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U_{0}(\cdot))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}\rightarrow 0.

For the third term on the right-hand side of (
4.7
), it follows from (
4.6
) that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g(x)))}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\big\|\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}((\min\{g_{h}(x),g(x)\},\,\max\{g_{h}(x),g(x)\}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{L}\Big(\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+1\big)\big(\tau^{2}+\nu^{-1}+2^{-\nu}\big)+2^{-\nu}\Big)x

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\big\|(U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-\underline{U},\,\mathcal{P}^{(\tau)}_{h}(x,0)(U^{\nu}_{0}(\cdot))-\underline{U})\big\|_{L^{\infty}((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}\big\|g^{\prime}_{h}(\cdot)-g^{\prime}(\cdot)\big\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow C\overline{L}\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+1\big)x\tau^{2},

as
\nu\rightarrow\infty
and
h\rightarrow 0
.

Now choose
C_{2}=C\overline{L}\big(\|U_{0}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}(\cdot)\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}+1\big)\tau^{2}
,

\varepsilon^{*}_{0}=\min\{\varepsilon_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}\}>0
, and
\tau^{*}_{0}=\min\{\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}_{0}\}>0
.
Then, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon^{*}_{0})
and
\tau\in(0,\tau^{*}_{0})
,
if
U_{0}
and
g(x)
satisfy (
1.21
), estimate (
1.26
) follows from the estimates
on the right-hand side of (
4.7
) altogether.
