I

Fig. 4.1:
The optimal convergence rate

Second, we show that the convergence rate in (
1.26
) is optimal.
To make this, it suffices to consider a special case.
As shown in Fig.
4.1
, suppose that the solution of the Riemann problem (
A.7
)
consists of only one
1^{\rm st}
shock-front
\alpha_{1}
with
strength
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
, which issues from point
(\hat{x},\hat{y})
,
with
U_{b}=(\rho_{b},u_{b},v_{b},p_{b})^{\top}=(1,0,\epsilon,\frac{1}{\gamma a^{2}_{\infty}})^{\top}

and
U_{a}=(\rho_{a},u_{a},v_{a},p_{a})^{\top}=(1+a_{\infty}\epsilon,\frac{\epsilon}{a_{\infty}},\epsilon,\frac{1}{\gamma a^{2}_{\infty}}+\frac{\epsilon}{a_{\infty}})^{\top}

as its
below
and
above
states for a constant
\epsilon>0
.

Note that, when
\epsilon=0
,
U_{b}=U_{a}=\underline{U}
.
Then, by
Lemma A.1
and the implicit function theorem, we know that the equation:

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{b})=U_{a}

(4.8)

admits a unique solution
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}(\epsilon)
for sufficiently small
\epsilon
with
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}(0)=0

and
\sigma^{\prime}_{\alpha_{1}}(0)=-\frac{\gamma+1}{2}
.
By the Taylor expansion theorem, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}(\epsilon)=-\frac{\gamma+1}{2}\epsilon+O(1)\epsilon^{2},

(4.9)

where the bound of function
O(1)
depends only on
\underline{U}
,
so that
\alpha_{1}
is a shock-front.
Let the speed of
\alpha_{1}
be
\mathcal{\dot{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{\dot{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})=\lambda_{1}(\underline{U})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)\sigma^{2}_{\alpha_{1}}=-\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)\sigma^{2}_{\alpha_{1}}.

(4.10)

We now consider the Riemann problem (
2.14
) with
below
state
U_{b}
and
above
state
U_{a}
.
Let
\beta_{j}
be the elementary wave-fronts of the solution with strength
\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}

for
1\leq j\leq 4
, that is,

\displaystyle\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{b}),

(4.11)

where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}\big)
,
and
\Phi^{(\tau)}
is defined by (
2.1
).

By Lemma
2.2
, for sufficiently small
\tau>0
, we have

\displaystyle\det\Big(\frac{\partial\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})}{\partial\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\Big)\bigg|_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{{}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}=0}

\displaystyle=\det\Big(\mathbf{r}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\underline{U},\tau^{2}),\mathbf{r}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\underline{U},\tau^{2}),\mathbf{r}^{(\tau)}_{3}(\underline{U},\tau^{2}),\mathbf{r}^{(\tau)}_{4}(\underline{U},\tau^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{8(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{7}{2}}}{(\gamma+1)^{2}a^{8}_{\infty}}<0.

Then it follows from the implicit function theorem and Lemma
2.3
that equation (
4.11
) admits
a unique solution
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, which is a function of
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
and
\tau^{2}
,
such that

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},0)=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\qquad\,\,\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},0)=0\quad\mbox{for $j=2,3,4$},\qquad

(4.12)

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(0,0)=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}(0,\tau^{2})=0\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

(4.13)

We now compute
\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}
for
1\leq j\leq 4
.
Taking the derivative on (
4.11
) with respect to
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum^{4}_{j=1}\frac{\partial\Phi^{(\tau)}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(\tau)}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}};U_{b},\tau^{2})}{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}\frac{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}=\frac{\partial\Phi(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{b})}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}.

(4.14)

Letting
\alpha_{1}=\tau=0
in (
4.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum^{4}_{j=1}\mathbf{r}_{j}(\underline{U},0)\frac{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=\mathbf{r}_{1}(\underline{U}),

(4.15)

so that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=1,\qquad\,\,\frac{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=0\quad\mbox{for $j=2,3,4$}.

(4.16)

Next, we take the derivative on (
4.14
) with respect to
\tau^{2}
and let
\alpha_{1}=\tau=0
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum^{4}_{j=1}\mathbf{r}_{j}(\underline{U},0)\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=-\sum^{4}_{j=1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{r}_{j}(\underline{U},\tau^{2})}{\partial\tau^{2}}\bigg|_{\tau=0}\frac{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{j}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}.

(4.17)

Substituting (
4.16
) into (
4.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=\frac{7}{4a^{2}_{\infty}},\qquad\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=\frac{1}{4a^{2}_{\infty}},

(4.18)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=0,\qquad\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{1}=\tau=0}=-\frac{4(a_{\infty}+1)}{(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}.\qquad

(4.19)

Therefore, by the Taylor formula, (
4.12
)–(
4.13
), and (
4.18
)–(
4.19
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},0)+\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(0,\tau^{2})-\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}(0,0)

\displaystyle\quad\ +\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{2}\int^{1}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}\frac{\partial^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\partial\tau^{2}}(\mu_{1}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\mu_{2}\tau^{2})\,{\rm d}\mu_{1}{\rm d}\mu_{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{7}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},

(4.20)

where the bound of function
O(1)
depends only on
\underline{U}
.

In the same way, we can also deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})=O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\\[2.0pt]
&\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})=-\frac{4(a_{\infty}+1)}{(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\\[2.0pt]
&\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})=\frac{1}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\end{split}

(4.21)

where the bound of functions
O(1)
depends only on
\underline{U}
.

Now, we consider the estimate of
\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}}
.
When
\epsilon
and
\tau
are sufficiently small, it follows from (
4
)–(
4.21
) that

\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{4}
are shock waves, and
\beta_{2}
and
\beta_{3}
are vortex sheets/entropy waves.
As shown in Fig.
4.1
, we denote
U^{(\tau)}_{m1}

and
U^{(\tau)}_{m2}
as the middle states, between waves
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
, and between waves
\beta_{3}
and
\beta_{4}
, respectively.
Then

\displaystyle U^{(\tau)}_{m1}=\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2}),\,\,\,U^{(\tau)}_{m2}=\Phi^{(\tau)}_{3}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}};\Phi^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}),

\displaystyle U_{a}=\Phi^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}};U^{(\tau)}_{m2},\tau^{2}).

(4.22)

Let
\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{k}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{k}},\tau^{2})
be the speed of wave
\beta_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 4
.
Note that, for
k=2,3
, waves
\beta_{2}
and
\beta_{3}
are vortex sheets/entropy waves so that their speeds satisfy

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})=\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{3}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\tau^{2})=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{3}(U^{(\tau)}_{m2},\tau^{2}).

In addition, it is direct to see that

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})<\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}},\tau^{2})<\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})=\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{3}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\tau^{2})<\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}},\tau^{2}).

For any
x>\hat{x}
, let
I_{x}:=\{(x,y)\,:\,y\in\mathbb{R}\}
.
As shown in Fig.
4.1
, let
I
,
II
and
III
be the intervals on
I_{x}
between waves
\beta_{1}
and
\alpha_{1}
,
\alpha_{1}
and
\beta_{2}(3)
,
and
\beta_{2}(3)
and
\beta_{4}
, respectively, issuing from point
(\hat{x},\hat{y})
.
From the construction, we see that

\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}(I_{x}\backslash(I\cup II\cup III))}\equiv 0.

Thus, it suffices to consider the comparison of the Riemann solutions on intervals
I
,
II
, and
III
in the following three cases:

Case 1
:
Estimate of
\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}(I)}
.
By direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(0,\tau^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=-\big(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}.

(4.23)

Then, if
\tau
and
\epsilon
are sufficiently small, it follows from (
4.10
) and (
4
) that

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\big(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}-\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big)+O(1)\tau^{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}+O(1)\big(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}-\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2a^{3}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)\tau^{2}>0.

(4.24)

Therefore,
I=\big\{(x,y)\,:\,\hat{y}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})(x-\hat{x})<y<\hat{y}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})(x-\hat{x})\big\}
,
so that the length of
I
is

\displaystyle|I|

\displaystyle=\big(\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}},\tau^{2})\big)(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2a^{3}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x}).

(4.25)

Note that
U^{(\tau)}=U^{(\tau)}_{m1}
and
U=U_{b}
on
I
.
Then
|U^{(\tau)}-U|=|U^{(\tau)}_{m1}-U_{b}|
on
I
.
We now estimate the term:
|U^{(\tau)}_{m1}-U_{b}|
.
By (
4.21
)
and direct computation, we have

\displaystyle|\rho^{(\tau)}_{m1}-\rho_{b}|

\displaystyle=\big|\Phi^{(\tau),(1)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2})-\rho_{b}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2\big((a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}-\tau^{2}\big)(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{2}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2a_{\infty}}{\gamma+1}\Big(1-\frac{2a_{\infty}+5}{2a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{7}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle\quad\,+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle=\frac{2a_{\infty}}{\gamma+1}\Big(1-\frac{4a_{\infty}+3}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|,

(4.26)

\displaystyle|u^{(\tau)}_{m1}-u_{b}|

\displaystyle=\big|\Phi^{(\tau),(2)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2})-u_{b}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}\Big(1+\frac{1}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|,

(4.27)

\displaystyle|v^{(\tau)}_{m1}-v_{b}|

\displaystyle=\big|\Phi^{(\tau),(3)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2})-v_{b}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{2}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\gamma+1}\Big(1-\frac{1}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|,

(4.28)

\displaystyle|p^{(\tau)}_{m1}-p_{b}|

\displaystyle=\big|\Phi^{(\tau),(4)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2})-p_{b}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}|+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}\Big(1+\frac{1}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|.

(4.29)

Therefore, it follows from (
4
)–(
4
) that

\displaystyle|U^{(\tau)}_{m1}-U_{b}|

\displaystyle=|\rho^{(\tau)}_{m1}-\rho_{b}|+|u^{(\tau)}_{m1}-u_{b}|+|v^{(\tau)}_{m1}-v_{b}|+|p^{(\tau)}_{m1}-p_{b}|

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2)}{(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}-\frac{4a^{3}_{\infty}+3a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}-2}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\bigg)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle\quad\ +O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|.

Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{I}|U^{(\tau)}-U|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=|U^{(\tau)}_{m1}-U_{b}||I|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2a^{3}_{\infty}}\Big(\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2)}{(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}-\frac{4a^{3}_{\infty}+3a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}-2}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle\quad+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x}).

(4.30)

Case 2
:
Estimate of
\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}(II)}
.
By (
4
), for sufficiently small
\tau
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2}),\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(\underline{U},\tau^{2})+\frac{\partial\lambda^{(\tau)}_{2}(\Phi^{(\tau)}_{1}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}};U_{b},\tau^{2}),\tau^{2})}{\partial\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}}\bigg|_{\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}=0}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{2}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{1}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\gamma+1}\Big(1-\frac{2}{a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{7}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}\Big)\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\,+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\gamma+1}\Big(1-\frac{\tau^{2}}{4a^{2}_{\infty}}\Big)\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|.

(4.31)

Then, by (
4.10
) and (
4.31
), we have

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2(\gamma+1)}\Big(\gamma+5-\frac{\tau^{2}}{a^{2}_{\infty}}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|>0,

(4.32)

so that
II=\big\{(x,y)\,:\,\hat{y}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}(x-\hat{x})<y<\hat{y}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}(x-\hat{x})\big\}
,
and its length is

\displaystyle|II|

\displaystyle=\big(\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}})\big)(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2(\gamma+1)}\Big(\gamma+5-\frac{\tau^{2}}{a^{2}_{\infty}}\Big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\Big)(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle\quad\,+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|(x-\hat{x}).

(4.33)

We now compute
|U^{(\tau)}-U|
on
II
. Set

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})=\Phi^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}};\Phi^{(\tau)}_{3}\big(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}};\Phi^{(\tau)}_{2}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}\big),\tau^{2}),

where
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}=(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}})
.

Denote
\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau),(k)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})
as the
k^{\rm th}
-component of
\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})
for
1\leq k\leq 4
.
Then it follows from
(
4.21
) that

\displaystyle|\rho_{a}-\rho^{(\tau)}_{m1}|

\displaystyle=\big|\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau),(1)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})-\rho^{(\tau)}_{m1}\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}}+\frac{2}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}\big((a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\tau^{2}\big)\big(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2}\big)^{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\,+O(1)\sum^{4}_{k=2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{8a_{\infty}+7}{2(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}.

(4.34)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle|u_{a}-u^{(\tau)}_{m1}|

\displaystyle=\big|\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau),(2)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})-u^{(\tau)}_{m1}\big|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{2}}+\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\sigma_{\beta_{4}}}\bigg|+O(1)\sum^{4}_{k=2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},

(4.35)

\displaystyle|v_{a}-v^{(\tau)}_{m1}|

\displaystyle=\big|\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau),(3)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})-v^{(\tau)}_{m1}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{2}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\sigma_{\beta_{4}}}|+O(1)\sum^{4}_{k=2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(\gamma+1)a^{2}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},

(4.36)

\displaystyle|p_{a}-p^{(\tau)}_{m1}|

\displaystyle=\big|\tilde{\Phi}^{(\tau),(4)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\tau)}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}};U^{(\tau)}_{m1},\tau^{2})-p^{(\tau)}_{m1}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2(a^{2}_{\infty}-\tau^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\sigma_{\beta_{4}}}|+O(1)\sum^{4}_{k=2}|\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{k}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}.

(4.37)

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle|U_{a}-U^{(\tau)}_{m1}|

\displaystyle=|\rho_{a}-\rho^{(\tau)}_{m1}|+|u_{a}-u^{(\tau)}_{m1}|+|v_{a}-v^{(\tau)}_{m1}|+|p_{a}-p^{(\tau)}_{m1}|

\displaystyle=\frac{8a^{3}_{\infty}+7a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}.

(4.38)

Then, by (
4.33
) and (
4.38
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{II}|U^{(\tau)}-U|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=|U^{(\tau)}-U||II|

\displaystyle=\frac{8a^{3}_{\infty}+7a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2}{2(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle\quad\,+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x}).

(4.39)

Case 3
:
Estimate of
\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}(III)}
. For sufficiently small
\tau
, by (
4.21
), we have

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{4}(0,\tau^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\big(a_{\infty}-\tau^{2}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}}+O(1)(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2a^{2}_{\infty}}\Big(1+\frac{a_{\infty}}{4}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\Big)\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|^{2}+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+\tau^{2}\big)\tau^{2}.

Then, by (
4.31
),

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{3}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\tau^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}-\frac{2}{\gamma+1}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{2a_{\infty}+\gamma+1}{(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\ +O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|^{2}+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|^{2}\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)>0,

(4.40)

so that
III=\{(x,y)\,:\,\hat{y}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{3}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\tau^{2})(x-\hat{x})<y<\hat{y}+\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}},\tau^{2})(x-\hat{x})\}
.
Then, by (
4
), its length is

\displaystyle|III|

\displaystyle=\big(\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}},\tau^{2})-\dot{\mathcal{S}}^{(\tau)}_{3}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{3}},\tau^{2})\big)(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{a_{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2a^{2}_{\infty}}\tau^{2}-\frac{2}{\gamma+1}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}+\frac{2a_{\infty}+\gamma+1}{(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}\tau^{2}\Big)(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle\quad\ +O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|^{2}+|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|^{2}\tau^{2}+\tau^{4}\big)(x-\hat{x}).

(4.41)

It follows from (
4
) that
|U^{(\tau)}-U|=|U_{a}-U^{(\tau)}_{m2}|
on
III
. Hence, by (
4.22
) and direct calculation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\rho_{a}-\rho^{(\tau)}_{m2}|&=\big|\Phi^{(\tau),(1)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}};U^{(\tau)}_{m2},\tau^{2})-\rho^{(\tau)}_{m2}\big|\\[1.0pt]
&=\frac{2}{2(\gamma+1)a_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\\[5.69054pt]
|u_{a}-u^{(\tau)}_{m1}|&=\big|\Phi^{(\tau),(2)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}};U^{(\tau)}_{m2},\tau^{2})-u^{(\tau)}_{m2}\big|\\[1.0pt]
&=\frac{3}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\\[5.69054pt]
|v_{a}-v^{(\tau)}_{m1}|&=\Big|\Phi^{(\tau),(3)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}};U^{(\tau)}_{m2},\tau^{2})-v^{(\tau)}_{m2}\Big|\\[1.0pt]
&=\frac{1}{2(\gamma+1)a^{2}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2},\\[5.69054pt]
|p_{a}-p^{(\tau)}_{m2}|&=\Big|\Phi^{(\tau),(4)}_{4}(\sigma^{(\tau)}_{\beta_{4}};U^{(\tau)}_{m2},\tau^{2})-p^{(\tau)}_{m2}\Big|\\[1.0pt]
&=\frac{3}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}.\end{split}

(4.42)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|U_{a}-U^{(\tau)}_{m2}|

\displaystyle=|\rho_{a}-\rho^{(\tau)}_{m2}|+|u_{a}-u^{(\tau)}_{m2}|+|v_{a}-v^{(\tau)}_{m2}|+|p_{a}-p^{(\tau)}_{m2}|

\displaystyle=\frac{6+a_{\infty}+a^{2}_{\infty}}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}.

(4.43)

By (
4
) and (
4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{III}|U_{a}-U^{(\tau)}_{m2}|\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=|U_{a}-U^{(\tau)}_{m2}||III|

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+6}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x}).

(4.44)

Finally, combining (
4.30
), (
4
), and (
4
) altogether, we have

\displaystyle\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}(I_{x})}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{I}+\int_{II}+\int_{III}\bigg)|U^{(\tau)}-U|\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2}{(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}+\frac{8a^{3}_{\infty}+7a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+2}{2(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}+\frac{a^{2}_{\infty}+a_{\infty}+6}{2(\gamma+1)a^{3}_{\infty}}\bigg)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle\quad\ +O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{9a^{3}_{\infty}+10a^{2}_{\infty}+9a_{\infty}+6}{2(\gamma+1)a^{4}_{\infty}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x})+O(1)\big(|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|+\tau^{2}\big)|\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}|\tau^{2}(x-\hat{x}).

(4.45)

Substitute (
4.9
) into (
4
) and let
\hat{x}=0
. Then

\displaystyle\|U^{(\tau)}-U\|_{L^{1}(I_{x})}=\frac{9a^{3}_{\infty}+10a^{2}_{\infty}+9a_{\infty}+6}{4a^{4}_{\infty}}\epsilon x\tau^{2}+O(1)\big(\epsilon+\tau^{2}\big)\epsilon x\tau^{2}.

Therefore, based on the special solution, we know the convergence rate in (
1.26
) is optimal.
This completes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
∎

Appendix A
Existence of Entropy Solutions of Problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)

In this appendix, we are concerned with the global existence of the entropy solutions of the initial-boundary value
problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
) around the
background solution

\underline{U}\doteq(1,0,0,\frac{1}{\gamma a^{2}_{\infty}})^{\top}

and
g\equiv 0
.
That is, we consider problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)
for the initial-boundary data
U_{0}
and
g(x)
, which are small perturbations of
U=\underline{U}
and
g\equiv 0
.
Because the argument is similar and shorter than the one for Problem I in §2, we skip the proof.
By direct calculation, the characteristic polynomial for system (
1.12
) is

\displaystyle(\lambda-v)^{2}\big((\lambda-v)^{2}-c^{2}\big)=0,

(A.1)

which admits four roots,
i.e.
, the eigenvalues of system (
1.12
):

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}(U)=v+(-1)^{j}c\ \ \mbox{for $j=1,4$},\qquad\,\,\,\lambda_{i}(U)=v\ \ \mbox{for $i=2,3$}.

(A.2)

The corresponding eigenvectors are

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{j}(U)=\frac{2}{\gamma+1}((-1)^{j}\frac{\rho}{c},\,(-1)^{j+1}\lambda_{j}(U),\,1,\,(-1)^{j}\rho c)^{\top}\quad\,\mbox{for $j=1,4$,}\quad

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{2}(U)=(0,1,0,0)^{\top},\quad\ \mathbf{r}_{3}(U)=(1,0,0,0)^{\top},

(A.4)

which satisfy

\nabla_{U}\lambda_{j}(U)\cdot\mathbf{r}_{j}(U)\equiv 1\ \,\mbox{for $j=1,4$},\qquad\,\nabla_{U}\lambda_{i}(U)\cdot\mathbf{r}_{i}(U)\equiv 0\ \,\mbox{for $i=2,3$}.

(A.5)

From (
A.5
), we know that the
1^{\rm st}
and
4^{\rm th}
characteristics fields are genuinely nonlinearity,
and the
2^{\rm nd}
and
3^{\rm th}
characteristics fields are linearly degenerate. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{(\tau)}_{j}(U,\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=\lambda_{j}(U),\,\,\,\,\mathbf{r}^{(\tau)}_{j}(U,\tau^{2})\big|_{\tau=0}=\mathbf{r}_{j}(U)\quad\,\,\,\mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

(A.6)

A.1
Riemann problems and local interaction estimates for system (
1.12
)

In this subsection, we first consider the following Riemann problem.

\begin{cases}\mbox{System}\,\eqref{eq:1.14},\\[5.0pt]
\left.U\right|_{x=\hat{x}_{0}}=\begin{cases}U_{a},\quad&y>\hat{y}_{0},\\[2.0pt]
U_{b},\quad&y<\hat{y}_{0}.\end{cases}\end{cases}

(A.7)

Based on (
A.5
), we can follow the ideas in
[
3
,
27
,
38
]
to show the solvability
of the Riemann problem (
A.7
).

Lemma A.1.

There exists a constant
\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}>0
depending only on
\underline{U}
such that,
for any given two constant states
U_{a},U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}}(\underline{U})
,
the Riemann problem (
A.7
) admits a unique entropy solution, with five constant states denoted by
U_{k},0\leq k\leq 4
,
and separated by shock waves or rarefaction waves for the genuinely nonlinear characteristics fields and
by vortex sheets/entropy waves for the linearly degenerate characteristics fields.
Moreover, there exists a constant
\tilde{\delta}_{1}>0
sufficiently small, depending only on
\underline{U}
, such that
the
j^{\rm th}

physical admissible wave
\alpha_{j}
in
\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}}(\underline{U})

can be parameterized by
\sigma_{\alpha_{j}}
as
\sigma_{\alpha_{j}}\mapsto\Phi_{j}(\sigma_{\alpha_{j}};U_{b})
,
where
\Phi_{j}\in C^{2}\big((-\tilde{\delta}_{1},\tilde{\delta}_{1})\times O_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}}(\underline{U})\big)
satisfies

\displaystyle U_{j}=\Phi_{j}(\sigma_{\alpha_{j}};U_{j-1}),\quad\,U_{0}=U_{b},\quad\,U_{4}=U_{a},

(A.8)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\Phi_{j}}{\partial\sigma_{\alpha_{j}}}\right|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{j}}=0}=\mathbf{r}_{j}(U_{b})\qquad\ \mbox{for $1\leq j\leq 4$}.

(A.9)

We call
\alpha_{j}
the rarefaction wave if
\sigma_{\alpha_{j}}>0
, which is denoted by
\mathcal{R}_{j}(U_{b})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}}(\underline{U})
for
j=1,4
.
We call
\alpha_{j}
the shock wave if
\sigma_{\alpha_{j}}<0
, which is denoted by
\mathcal{S}_{j}(U_{b})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}}(\underline{U})
for
j=1,4
.
We call
\alpha_{k}
the vortex sheet/entropy wave if
\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}\neq 0
, which is denoted by
\mathcal{C}_{k}(U_{b})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{1}}(\underline{U})
for
k=2,3
.
Then, based on Lemma A.1
and by the standard method as done in
[
3
,
38
]
, similar to Lemma
2.3
,
we have the following local interaction estimates:

Lemma A.2.

There exists a constant
\tilde{\epsilon}_{2}\in(0,\tilde{\epsilon}_{1})
sufficiently small, depending on
\underline{U}
, such that,
for given three constant states
U_{a},U_{m},U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{2}}(\underline{U})
with

\displaystyle U_{a}=\Phi_{i}(\sigma_{\alpha_{i}};U_{m}),\quad U_{m}=\Phi_{k}(\sigma_{\beta_{j}};U_{b}),

\displaystyle U_{a}=\Phi(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}};U_{b}),\quad\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(\sigma_{\gamma_{1}},\sigma_{\gamma_{2}},\sigma_{\gamma_{3}},\sigma_{\gamma_{4}}),

then

\displaystyle\sigma_{\gamma_{k}}=\delta_{ki}\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}+\delta_{kj}\sigma_{\beta_{j}}+O(1)Q(\sigma_{\alpha_{i}},\sigma_{\beta_{j}})\qquad\,\mbox{for $1\leq k\leq 4$};

(A.10)

if three is a non-physical wave
\gamma_{\mathcal{NP}}
based on the construction, then

\displaystyle\sigma_{\gamma_{\mathcal{NP}}}=O(1)Q(\sigma_{\alpha_{i}},\sigma_{\beta_{j}}),

(A.11)

where

Q(\sigma_{\alpha_{i}},\sigma_{\beta_{j}})=\begin{cases}0\quad&\emph{if}\ i>j\quad\emph{or}\ i=j\ \emph{and}\ \min\{\sigma_{\alpha_{i}},\sigma_{\beta_{j}}\}>0,\\[5.0pt]
|\sigma_{\alpha_{i}}||\sigma_{\beta_{j}}|\quad&\emph{otherwise},\end{cases}

(A.12)

and the bound of function
O(1)
depends only on
\underline{U}
.

\textsc{C}_{1}
\textsc{C}_{2}
\textsc{C}_{3}
\alpha_{1}
U_{\Gamma_{2}}

Fig. 1.1:
Mixed Riemann problem for system (
1.12
)

Next, we consider the following mixed Riemann problem:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}{\rm System}~\eqref{eq:1.14}\quad&\mbox{in $\Omega_{2}$},\\[2.0pt]
U=U_{b}\quad&\mbox{on $\{x=x_{2}\}\cap\Omega_{2}$},\\[2.0pt]
v=\tan\theta_{2}\quad&\mbox{on $\Gamma_{2}$},\end{cases}

(A.13)

where
U_{b}
is a constant state near
\underline{U}
and satisfies

\displaystyle v_{b}=\tan\theta_{1},

(A.14)

and
\Omega_{k}
,
\theta_{k}
, and
x_{k}
are given as in (
2.18
)–(
2.19
) for
k=1,2
;
see Fig.
1.1
.
Then, similar to Lemma
2.4
, we have the following lemma on the solvability of the mixed Riemann problem (
A.13
):

Lemma A.3.

There exists a small constant
\tilde{\epsilon}_{3}>0
depending only on
\underline{U}
such that,
if
U_{b}\in\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{3}}(\underline{U})
and
|\omega|+|\theta_{1}|<\tilde{\epsilon}_{3}
,
then problem (
A.13
) admits a unique solution consisting of states
U_{\Gamma_{2}}
and
U_{b}
,
which are connected by a weak
1^{\rm st}
wave
\alpha_{1}
with strength
\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}
:

\displaystyle U_{\Gamma_{2}}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\alpha_{1}};U_{b}),

(A.15)

satisfying

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha_{1}}=K_{b}\omega,

(A.16)

where
\omega=\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}
, and the bound of
K_{b}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}
.

\Omega_{2}

\beta_{1}
U_{b}
U^{-}_{\Gamma_{2}}
U^{+}_{\Gamma_{2}}
x_{2}
x_{3}

Fig. 1.2:
Weak waves hit on the boundary and reflect

Finally, similar to Lemma
2.5
, we consider the weak wave reflection on the boundary (see also Fig.
1.2
).

Lemma A.4.

There is a constant
\tilde{\epsilon}_{4}>0
depending only on
\underline{U}
such that,
if
U^{-}_{\Gamma_{2}}
,
U_{b}\in O_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{4}}(\underline{U})
are two constant states and satisfy

v^{-}_{\Gamma_{2}}=\tan\theta_{2},\qquad U^{-}_{\Gamma_{2}}=\Phi_{\ell}(\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}};U_{b})\quad\mbox{for $\ell=2,3,4$},

then, for the constant state
U^{+}_{\Gamma_{2}}\in O_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{3}}(\underline{U})
with

\displaystyle v^{+}_{\Gamma_{2}}=\tan\theta_{2},\qquad U^{+}_{\Gamma_{2}}=\Phi_{1}(\sigma_{\beta_{1}};U_{b}),

the following estimate holds
:

\sigma_{\beta_{1}}=K_{r,\ell}\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}\qquad\mbox{for {$2\leq\ell\leq 4$}},

(A.17)

where the reflection coefficients
K_{r,\ell}
are
C^{2}
-functions of
(\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}},U_{b})
and satisfy

K_{r,4}|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{4}}=0,U_{b}=\underline{U}}=1,\qquad K_{r,\ell}|_{\sigma_{\alpha_{\ell}}=0,U_{b}=\underline{U}}=0\quad\mbox{for $\ell=2,3$}.

(A.18)

A.2
Entropy solutions of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)

In this subsection, we construct the approximate solutions of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)
via the wave-front tracking scheme, which is similar to the argument in §2.3.
Then the entropy solutions are obtained by passing the limit.
As done in §2.3, we first choose a parameter
\nu>0
and then approximate the initial data
U_{0}
by
U^{\nu}_{0}
,
with finite discontinuities, satisfying (
2.34
).
Then we choose a mesh length
h
and approximate the boundary function
g(x)
by
g_{h}(x)
, with piecewise constant slope,
satisfying (
2.30
).
Thus, the corresponding domain
\Omega
is approximated by
\Omega_{h}
with
\Gamma_{h}
as its boundary.
The approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
),
via the wave-front tracking scheme corresponding to
h
and
\nu
, is constructed in the following way:

As done in §2.3, we introduce two types of Riemann solvers,
(ARS)
and
(SRS)
,
for which the non-physical wave is introduced for
(SRS)
.
Moreover, a threshold parameter
\varrho>0
given in §2.3 is also introduced here.
Following the procedure in §2.3, if the product of the strengths of the two approaching wave-fronts
is larger than
\varrho
, then the accurate Riemann solver
(ARS)
is used.
Otherwise, the simplified Riemann solver
(SRS)
is used.
Now we solve the Riemann problem at each discontinuity via either
(ARS)
or
(SRS)

and continue this procedure for
x>0
.

Let
\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}\cup\mathcal{NP}
represent the wave-fronts in
U_{h,\nu}
.
In order to define the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
globally for all
x>0
,
one needs to show that the total number of the wave-fronts
\mathcal{J}
is finite.
This is done once the uniform bound on the total variation of
U_{h,\nu}
is obtained.
To achieve this, we follow the argument in §2.3 by introducing a weighted Glimm-type functional.

First, we make some
a priori
assumptions on the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
.

\mathbf{(\widetilde{P1})}

The approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
has been defined
for
x<\hat{x}
and satisfies

U_{h,\nu}(\hat{x}-,\cdot)\in\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{*}}(\underline{U})

for

\tilde{\epsilon}_{*}\in(0,\min\{\tilde{\epsilon}_{2},\tilde{\epsilon}_{3},\tilde{\epsilon}_{4}\})
;

\mathbf{(\widetilde{P2})}

For
x<\hat{x}
, the strength
\sigma_{\alpha}
of each front
\alpha
satisfies

|\sigma_{\alpha}|\leq O(1)\nu^{-1}
.

Then, for
x>0
, we define the modified Glimm-type functional as

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(x)=\mathcal{V}(x)+\mathcal{K}\mathcal{Q}(x),

(A.19)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(x)=\mathcal{V}_{1}(x)+\sum^{4}_{i=2}\mathcal{K}_{i}\mathcal{V}_{i}(x)+\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{NP}}(x)+\mathcal{K}_{c}\mathcal{V}_{c}(x),

(A.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{i}(x)=\sum_{\alpha_{i}\in\mathcal{J}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{i}}|\,\,\,\,\mbox{for $1\leq i\leq 4$},\qquad\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{NP}}(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{NP}}|\sigma_{\alpha}|,

(A.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{c}(x)=\sum_{k>[\frac{x}{h}]}|\omega_{k}|,\quad\mathcal{Q}(x)=\sum_{(\alpha_{i},\beta_{j})\in\mathcal{A}}|\sigma_{\alpha_{i}}||\sigma_{\beta_{j}}|.

(A.22)

Here
\mathcal{A}
stands for the set of all approaching waves; that is, for the
i^{\rm th}
wave
\alpha_{i}
and
j^{\rm th}
wave
\beta_{j}

with strengths
\sigma_{\alpha_{i}}
and
\sigma_{\beta_{j}}
and
located at
y_{\alpha_{i}}
and
y_{\beta_{j}}
, respectively, so that
y_{\alpha_{i}}<y_{\beta_{j}}
and one of the following conditions holds:

(i)

\alpha_{i}\in\mathcal{NP}
and
\beta_{j}\in\mathcal{J}\setminus\mathcal{NP}
,

(ii)

i>j
,

(iii)

i=j
and
\min\{\sigma_{\alpha_{i}},\sigma_{\beta_{j}}\}<0
.

Now, following a similar argument to the proof of Lemma
2.7
, we obtain

Lemma A.5.

There exist constants
\mathcal{K}_{i}
for
i=2,3,4
,
\mathcal{K}_{c}
, and
\mathcal{K}
depending only
on
\underline{U}
, and a small constant
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
depending only on
\tilde{\epsilon}_{*}
and
\underline{U}

such that, if
\mathcal{G}(\hat{x}-)<\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon\in(0,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\hat{x}+)<\mathcal{G}(\hat{x}-)\qquad\mbox{for $\hat{x}>0$},

and
U_{h,\nu}(\hat{x}+,\cdot)\in\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\epsilon}_{*}}(\underline{U})
.
Moreover, at
x=\hat{x}+
, strength
\sigma_{\alpha}
of each front
\alpha\in\mathcal{J}
satisfies
|\sigma_{\alpha}|\leq O(1)\nu^{-1}
.

Based on Lemma A.5
and by an induction procedure as in
[
3
,
30
]
,
we can obtain the uniform
BV\cap L^{\infty}
bound of the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
.

Proposition A.1.

Under assumptions
(\mathbf{U_{0}})
and
(\mathbf{g})
, there exists a constant
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}=\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}(\tilde{\epsilon},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})>0

depending only on
\underline{U}
such that, for some
\varepsilon\in(0,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1})
, if the initial data
U_{0}
and
the boundary function
g(x)
satisfy (
2.46
),
then the approximate solution
U_{h,\nu}
generated by the wave-front tracking scheme can be defined globally for all
x>0
with
the properties that
U_{h,\nu}\in(BV_{\rm loc}\cap L^{1}_{\rm loc})(\Omega_{h})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x>0}\big\|U_{h,\nu}(x,\cdot)-\underline{U}\big\|_{BV((-\infty,g_{h}(x)))}

\displaystyle\,\leq{C_{A,1}}\big(\|U_{0}-\underline{U}\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big),

(A.23)

and, for any
\tilde{x}^{\prime},\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\big\|U_{h,\nu}(\tilde{x}^{\prime},\cdot+g_{h}(\tilde{x}^{\prime}))-U_{h,\nu}(\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime},\cdot+g_{h}(\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}))\big\|_{L^{1}((-\infty,0))}\leq{C_{A,2}}|\tilde{x}^{\prime}-\tilde{x}^{\prime\prime}|,

(A.24)

where constants
C_{A,1}>0
and
C_{A,2}>0
depend only on
\underline{U}
.
The strength of each rarefaction-front is small
:

\displaystyle|\sigma_{\alpha}|\leq{C_{A,3}}\nu^{-1}\qquad\mbox{for $\alpha\in\mathcal{R}$}.

(A.25)

The total strength of nonphysical waves is small,
i.e.
, there exists a positive threshold
\varrho=\varrho_{\nu}>0

with
\varrho_{\nu}\rightarrow 0
as
\nu\rightarrow\infty

such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{NP}}|\sigma_{\alpha}|\leq{C_{A,4}}2^{-\nu},

(A.26)

where constants
C_{A,3}>0
and
C_{A,4}>0
depend only on
\underline{U}
.

By Proposition A.1
and Theorem 2.4 in
[
3
]

and following the arguments in
[
9
,
24
,
30
,
38
,
41
]
,
we can obtain the compactness for the convergence of the approximate solutions
\{U_{h,\nu}\}
.

Proposition A.1.

Under assumptions
(\mathbf{U_{0}})
and
(\mathbf{g})
,
there exists a subsequence
\{U_{h_{k},\nu_{k}}\}
obtained
by the wave-front tracking scheme such that
U_{h_{k},\nu_{k}}\rightarrow U
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\Omega)
as
h_{k}\rightarrow 0
and
\nu_{k}\rightarrow\infty

for
k\rightarrow\infty
such that

U\in(BV_{loc}\cap L^{1}_{\rm loc})(\Omega)
is the global entropy solution of the initial-boundary value problem (
1.11
)–(
1.13
)
in the sense of
Definition
1.1
for
\tau=0
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{x>0}\big\|U(x,\cdot)-\underline{U}\big\|_{BV((-\infty,g(x)))}\leq{C_{A,5}}\big(\|U_{0}-\underline{U}\|_{BV(\mathcal{I})}+|g^{\prime}(0)|+\|g^{\prime}\|_{BV(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\big),

where
C_{A,5}>0
depends only on
\underline{U}
.
